A private beta at routemaker.cieply.com for trusted friends and family, on the owner's shared server: 360 "It's going to be on beta and not shared outside of trusted friends and family.", 362 "All four routers", and 367's answers on TLS, concurrency and stripping users (Owner amendments, below).

  Implemented as (wip/beta, docs/BETA-RUNBOOK.md for the server's operator): a compose overlay, `compose.beta.yaml` (no caddy or rebuild, the api alone published on 127.0.0.1, hard memory caps of 6.94 GiB resident, six gunicorn workers, binds that never create their source), gated by `scripts/check_beta_compose.py` and run only through `scripts/beta/beta-compose.sh`; one new nginx site file rendered from `deploy/beta/nginx-routemaker.conf.template` (basic auth, noindex, a small sign-in page); `scripts/beta/ship-data.sh` and `receive-data.sh` (tiles, Photon index, base map and a dump without identities, sha-checked; the stress-tile cache is drawn on the server); the beta notice (`frontend/src/lib/betaBanner.ts`, VITE_BETA=1); and a tester handout, `docs/BETA-TESTER-HANDOUT.md`.

- **FOLLOWUP-SIDEBAR-REDESIGN** (312, and 313 and 324 defer to it). Approved as the target layout ("That works."); front end only, after the release.

  Implemented as (wip/sidebar, front end only; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md "The sidebar (312)"): Points first, with the how-to behind "More tips" and one lede line that keeps the keyboard's way in; the ride settings behind a one-line Ride summary with Edit (ride type, traffic and hills, when, a set target distance, loop, avoid gravel; never the weight); with a route, the points compact behind "Edit points", and the summary shows the totals, the stress bar (its shares in words) and four quick figures, with Stress and facilities, Directions, Junctions to watch and Routes to choose from as folds (the last open by default); Download GPX and Copy link pinned under the scrolling part (Copy link, new to the code, copies this page with the plan's fragment from encodePlan, never the weight); a bottom bar (Map layers, Legend, GPX, Settings; About until 384) whose sheets replace the planner in the panel, the Map layers sheet holding the switches in 312's order and the full legend (including the junction markers, always in view), with the zoom explanations behind "What each zoom level shows". On a short or zoomed screen the whole panel scrolls as one. Where it differs from the approved layout:
  - A "Stress and facilities" fold sits in the elevation slot; there is no elevation chart, which is FOLLOWUP-ELEVATION-CHART (322). Since built (wip/elevation-chart): the "Elevation and stress" fold takes the slot, first, and "Stress and facilities" stays after it, because the stress breakdown has nowhere else in 312's layout (decided, 394: "keep both").
  - The panel follows the system theme rather than forcing the dark colours; its dark colours are the mockup's (decided, 384: "system").
  - The zoom notice is in the Map layers sheet only; a one-line planner zoom notice and a High contrast shortcut in the planner are built and switched off (`PLANNER_EXTRAS`; decided off by 384, items 3 and 4).
  - The font is on (384, item 5): frontend/src/fonts/fonts.css, relative url()s that Vite fingerprints into /assets/, imported by main.tsx, with the two font files and the SIL OFL licence beside it.
  - 324's separate Mass Ride layers variant is not built: there is one Map layers sheet, with federal land shown only for a Mass Ride and a line saying Mass Ride has its own layers; the variant waits on FOLLOWUP-MASSRIDE-MAP (325-334).
  - The weight dialog's layout (313) is unchanged.
  - GPX stays in the bottom bar with a route shown (the mockup drops it to three buttons), so "Open GPX…" is always reachable.
- **FOLLOWUP-ELEVATION-CHART** (322, 323). "A route view in most cases should have the option to show a chart of elevation, with steep slopes highlighted and stress on a rolling basis." Approved as drawn ("looks good."), to build with the sidebar redesign.

  Implemented as (wip/elevation-chart; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md "The route chart"): the API's route answer gains `profile` (`core.api.ProfileOut`, `routemaker.profile`), parallel arrays from the router's own per-leg elevation samples, one every 30 m (`routing.ELEVATION_INTERVAL_M`; 3DEP at 1 arc-second is about 30 m, so a finer sampling would read one cell twice): metres along the route, the height, a grade read across two samples either side (120 m, because the grade between two neighbours is mostly DEM noise: 3 m over 30 m is 10%), and the sustained climbs (`routemaker.climbs.runs`, listed where they average 3% or reach 5%), each with its highest stress tier. The chart is SVG in React with no dependency (`frontend/src/ElevationChart.tsx`; the decisions are in `lib/profileChart.ts`): distance across in miles with kilometres in brackets, elevation up in feet with metres in brackets, the 5% to 8% band amber with dark dots and the 8%-or-more band amber with a dark hatch (a pattern each, so the amber is never the only cue), and the rolling stress strip under it in the map's colours (a traffic-free path and an unpaved stretch drawn and said as the map has them) with the stress bar's patterns. The picture is one slider (role slider, one tab stop): the arrow keys step along the route (the smallest of 0.1, 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2 or 5 miles that crosses it in 80 steps or fewer), Page keys step five, Home and End go to the ends, C and Shift+C go to the next and previous climb, and its value text is the sentence "Mile 4.2: elevation 310 ft (94 m), grade 6%, LTS 2", which follows the keyboard (or a click) only, never a moving mouse; hovering or touching says the same and a ringed, cross-marked marker follows on the map (`MapView` `scrubPoint`). The text alternative is a summary, and the climbs as a table (start mile, length, gain, average and maximum grade, stress) behind "Climbs as a table". The fold is closed on a small screen and open beside the map. Where it differs from the mock-up: the fold sits above "Stress and facilities" and does not replace it (see the 312 note; decided, 394: "keep both"); the readout line under the chart is hidden from a screen reader, because the slider's value text is the announcement and a live region beside it would say it twice; the Mountain Goat list can reuse `climbRows`, but is not wired to it.
- **FOLLOWUP-MASSRIDE-MAP** (325-334). "This needs a redesign. The focus is on carrying capacity, not LTS here." Approved as drawn ("Looks good"), after the release, with the sidebar redesign and the elevation chart.

  Implemented as, part 2 only (387: the Mass Ride route chart; wip/elevation-chart; the capacity map, part 1, and the hazards, part 3, are not built): the same chart with the riders-per-minute area in place of the stress strip (332), and the flow model it needs. There was no flow model in the code, only in this PLAN's "The headline number: modelled throughput", so `routemaker.flow` is that model, accepted as the working model (394); sources pending FOLLOWUP-FLOW-CALIBRATION. The level figure rests on the owner's DC Bike Party counts (item 173, indicative, about ±25%, item 175); the grade factors are not from a measurement. Riders a minute = 0.37 riders/m2 x 0.7 utilisation x usable width x 1.9 m/s (about 99 for an 11 ft (3.35 m) lane), the width from the segment's lanes (`road_lanes`, `road_oneway`, both directions of a two-way street, one lane a direction where the table has none, about 10 ft (3.0 m) for a path, none for a stretch marked Avoid, 325), and the grade adjustment of 328(b): a climb slows the group to 1 / (1 + 12 x (grade - 1%)) of its pace (63% at 6%, 54% at 8%, never under 30%), taking 150 m of climbing to set in so a short ramp costs little, and a descent past 4% spaces the group at 1 / (1 + 5 x (grade - 4%)) of the density (83% at 8%, never under 60%). The route answer's `profile` carries `riders_per_min` for every sample, `flow` (the narrowest point and the median), each climb's `capacity_drop_pct` and `min_riders_per_min`, the stretches marked Avoid (`avoid`) and the untraced ones (`unchecked`), and `crossings`: the major junctions from `routemaker.intersections.major_crossings`, as 396 defines them - the crossed or joined road is LTS 3 or higher whatever the control, or the junction carries a stress rating; no lane counting and no stop-sign rule, and a signalised right onto an arterial or a busy cross street passed along a busy road is marked though group mode makes no event of it - each with its street, the planner's marker (orange triangle, red diamond, or a dot for a busy road crossed or joined with no planner flag), why it is major (`kind`), and `corkers_needed` from the crossed or joined road's tier (142, 400). `crossings` is null where the junctions were not read (over budget, or the reads failed): "not checked", never "none". In the chart: the area filled in the 327 colours with a pattern in each band (cross-hatch, diagonal, dots, horizontal lines), cut where the line crosses 60, 120 or 200, dotted guides whose figures are in the text colour beside a band-colour swatch (3:1 lines; 329's "labelled in the band colours" kept on the line and the swatch), its own axis, the narrowest point marked with a downward triangle and its figure (147), Avoid stretches drawn and said as Avoid with no figure (325), a tick and marker at each major junction with its street name, names that would collide thinned in favour of the more stressful, and tables of the climbs, the bottlenecks (under 60) and every intersection. The scrub says "Mile 1.1: grade 6%, about 90 riders per minute (tight, slowed by the climb). Next: 14th St at mile 1.3, corkers needed." (I and Shift+I jump between intersections). Not done: the map line's colours stay as they are (part 1), except that the route's own Avoid sections are magenta (397); the skull marker waits for 307; 329's line is superseded by 332's area, as decided; and two parts of 147's "Route profile" that need the ride-size slider: the ride-size requirement line on the flow track, and the running count of corkers needed at once.
- **FOLLOWUP-FLOW-CALIBRATION** (394). "We have literature to check that plus video recordings of events in DC that seem to indicate that it's a reasonable number." `routemaker.flow` is accepted as the working model; check its constants (the level figure's density, utilisation and pace, and the grade factors, which are not from a measurement) against the literature and the owner's DC event videos, then record the sources in docs/SOURCES.md ("The Mass Ride flow model", pending until then). A constant that changes moves the chart at once; part 1's capacity map stores the physical width, so it follows without a rebuild.
- **FOLLOWUP-ISECT-AVOID** (307-310, 335). "we should also have an avoid rating for intersections. There's some that are just way too problematic. Would only be added through community (or my) input." Queued after the release, alongside STRESS-SUGGEST.
- **FOLLOWUP-WORKZONES** (341-349). "We should separate construction into 2 parts: where the road is rideable but would have rough surfaces or hazards, it should get some penalty, and where the road is closed, route around." Sources approved per 342, 346-349; not built.

**Future release** (named by the owner, after the next one):

- **FOLLOWUP-HILLS-TOLERATE** (242). "I'm wondering for future releases if it wouldn't make sense for the hill slider, outside mountain goat to not nessicarily perfer hills, but mostly tolerate them. For instance lighter individuals would probably move the slider to more hilly as it's faster than them. People with ebikes would probably just want a more direct route as hills wouldn't affect them too much." On every ride type except Mountain Goat, the right half of the hills slider would mean "hills don't bother me": it discounts climbing time and favours the shorter or more direct route, instead of seeking climbs. Only Mountain Goat keeps seek-hills, the climb search and FOLLOWUP-HILL-SEEK (205). The E-bike ride type would start toward tolerate, and the slider's words would change to match ("Avoids hills" to "Fine with hills"). Planner and costing only, no rebuild expected; Valhalla's `use_hills` floor (1.0) may need a time discount in the refine score.
- **FOLLOWUP-BIKESHARE** (243, 244, 245). "Another one to add to the plan would be bikeshare. The data is available here. There are two forms of bikes, the classic doesn't have a lot of gears so would be less hill tolerant, and an ebike version with less of an issue. We'd want the searches to find the nearest dock and route from dock to dock." Also: "Realtime station availabilities might even be a good option too". Data: Open Data DC "Capital Bikeshare Locations", and the GBFS 1.1 feeds (gbfs.capitalbikeshare.com; station_information, station_status, ebikes_at_stations, system_alerts). Design, not built: two bike types, Classic (few gears, hill-averse, heavier, slower) and E-bike (hills of little concern, assisted speed); the plan walks from the start to the nearest suitable dock (Valhalla pedestrian costing, if the tiles have pedestrian access), rides dock to dock, then walks to the destination; live availability needs a bike of the chosen type at the start dock and a free slot at the end dock, falls back to the next-nearest dock, and says so when availability is unknown; station locations come from GBFS station_information, which covers every jurisdiction, not the DC-only layer; station_status is cached server-side for about 60 s; planning stays anonymous. A licence check comes first: Capital Bikeshare and Lyft's data licence for GBFS and the Open Data DC layer's licence (neither metadata response stated one). Item 244: "Ebikes can be parked outside of a dock, but carry an additional fee. There's also areas where parking outside of it carries a fine." Design addition, not built: for E-bike the plan offers two endings, at the nearest dock with a free slot or at the destination outside a dock with the out-of-dock fee shown (read from GBFS system_pricing_plans, never hard-coded); it never suggests ending in a no-parking or fine zone (GBFS geofencing_zones if published, else the operator's zone map, licence checked; with no zone data it suggests docks only); a free-floating e-bike (GBFS free_bike_status) can be the start; fees and fines are shown as the operator's information, not a quote. Item 245, on 244: "And can be retrieved outside of a dock." confirms the free-floating start: the planner weighs the nearest free-floating e-bike against docks and takes the shorter walk plus ride. Related: the nearest-dock hint for bail-out points.

**Backlog** (to do, not scheduled; item 148: "Most of these are to do, that's a backlog."):

- **FOLLOWUP-HILL-SEEK** (205). "Yes, add it to the backlog." A candidate generator that finds climbs near the line and routes through them, like the trail seek. Today, right of the detent, the planner only picks the most-climbing of the router's three alternatives within the distance budget.
- **FOLLOWUP-TANDEM** (221). "Backlog it (Recommended)". A tandem option: heavier on climbs, wider turns, avoids tight barriers, bollards and narrow path gates, longer stopping. Item 220's stokers are its riders, and item 241's rationale applies to them: a rider who is not in control of the ride (the stoker) is stress-averse.
- **FOLLOWUP-DECIMAL-STRESS** (150, 156, 180). 150: "Would an LTS 2.5 make sense to include? We could also have decimal numbers of traffic stress that only affect routing.", then "Yes, let's add to a backlog". 156: "When we incorporate all of these, let's think about building fractional levels too. we should have a set of roads that includes width traffic bike facilities, etc and could probably make a decent regression model." A fractional stress for routing only, from an ordinal regression on the agency LTS layers and the owner's curated tiers, keeping LTS 4 at least twice LTS 3; the map, legend and summary keep whole levels. Item 240's (C), graded LTS 2 in the routing graph, belongs here. 180 adds Montgomery's gap (1,558 mi of residential streets rated LTS 2 here and LTS 1 by Montgomery, reports/data-comparison/moco-lts-comparison.md) as an input. It needs its own rebuild and before/after checks.
- **FOLLOWUP-BOOT.** A boot-safe startup: Docker Desktop starts the containers before the Ubuntu bind mounts exist on every Windows reboot, so postgis can start on an empty cluster (docs/OPERATIONS.md, "After a host restart: the postgis bind race", is the manual procedure until then). Candidate: compose restart set to no, plus a WSL systemd user unit that waits for the binds and then brings the services up in order, postgis first, checking the migrations.
- **FOLLOWUP-SIGNAL-DERIVE.** Derive the signal of a mapped crossing way (`highway=crossing` with `crossing=traffic_signals`, a beacon) in the tag transform, so a signalised trail crossing away from a signalised road junction reaches the router's signal flag instead of reading "no signal mapped" (Routing model, "Known gaps"). It needs a rebuild to verify.
- **Alexandria 20 mph** (183), blocked pending a public basis. "Apply Alexandria 20 mph", sourced from the CC0 or OSM side, never from the internal-only Transport Streets data. Not implemented: no public basis is in the repository (Owner amendments, 2026-10-01, Agency data). It needs the city's ordinance or policy setting 20 mph on residential streets, its URL or text from the owner, after which it is a default like the District's.
- **A dedicated MTB mode** (90, 111, 148). 111: "We will need a whole dedicated MTB mode. There's a whole set of criteria there." 148: "Mountain bike mode is much later." Its own graph or variant seeking singletrack by `mtb:scale` and the rest of the MTB criteria, which every current ride type closes (Owner amendments, Mountain-bike singletrack).
- **The GPX far-apart-parts import fix.** A file whose tracks or segments lie far apart, or that the coverage box clips into several runs, is joined into one line, and the straight jump between the parts is then followed as track: the page freezes and the plan bends (reports GPX-review-r1, should-fix 1). Export is unaffected.
- **Elevation in exports.** The GPX export carries no `<ele>`: it needs an elevation profile from the API first.
- **The stationBikeEntrance naming.** A point placed at a station's bike entrance ("Add as stop" on a station card) is named by the router for the entrance's street ("7th Street Northwest"), not for the station; the place search has still to adopt `stationBikeEntrance` and name such points after the station.

## Owner amendments

The amendments are in date order, then by OWNER-DECISIONS item number (the record, OWNER-DECISIONS-2026-09-26b.md, is outside the repository). A later item that amends an earlier one has its own entry, and the earlier entry points to it; a sentence a later item overtook is marked "superseded" where it stands.

**2026-09-27.** Recorded in the owner's words, each followed by what it changes above.

- Sliders. "Also, in addition to the default and mass rides, lets have sliders where we can adjust the penalties for traffic stress and hilliness. The lowest setting for traffic stress is fastest legal route to basically one where low stress routes are used unless there's no other option. For hilliness, we can go from a default which is basically fastest time for most people in the middle, to hill avoidant, and even hill seeking." The stress slider is `use_roads` run backwards (0 is 1.0, 100 is 0.0), and the stress tiers reach the graph as a use_roads-weighted penalty on LTS 3-4 ways rather than as a cycleway write on LTS 1, so one graph serves every position. The hills slider's middle is `use_hills` 1.0, no grade penalty and so the fastest time under Valhalla's grade-speed model; below it `use_hills` falls to 0; above it the climb search is, for now, a choice among the router's alternatives within a distance budget, two-point plans only, rather than the layer 4 via-point search described under Mountain Goat. Default's `use_hills` is therefore 1.0 rather than mid.
- Default's stress position. "It seems that the current default is probably a bit too traffic tolerant for most people. For instance my route to work, would typically be about 2 hrs on full trails. Instead it puts me on a set of roads where almost a quarter are LTS4." And of the faster road route: "It is faster, but not worth it for most people." Default starts well toward quiet (stress 90, use_roads 0.10; see "Default at 90"), not at mid; the fastest-legal behaviour belongs at the slider's low end.
- Traffic-free paths. "We might also want a separate category for traffic free paths." It is the first of the facility classes below, not a fifth stress tier: the stress tiers are unchanged, and a route reports metres per facility class alongside metres per tier.
- Facilities. "Also, show and perfer protected bike lanes and off road bike paths. They should get a bump for routing: Basically the preference goes here: Off-road bike lanes > Protected Bike Lanes >> Marked Bike Lanes > Ordinary Streets. Sharrows don't count as anything." Each segment carries one of path, protected, lane, none, computed once at the rebuild and read by routing, the breakdown and the map. Routing prices painted lanes near ordinary streets and sharrows as ordinary streets; a separately mapped protected lane (`highway=cycleway`) and an off-road cycleway tie, because Valhalla prices both as closed to pedestrians and only an access tag could separate them.
- Mass Ride and facilities. "Mass rides don't need to consider these. Even protected bike lanes aren't used." The no-trail variant carries no facility preference at all: its roadways lose their cycle-lane classes in the graph, and separately mapped lanes are trail-class and already absent from it.
- Car-free roads. "There are a couple roads like beach drive in Montgomery County that are closed to car traffic on the weekends. These should be considered off road paths then. (most of beach drive in DC is closed to car traffic permanantly)" A road closed to motor vehicles outright is a path. A road closed at set times is a path in the breakdown for a ride inside the closure; the graph holds one state per way, so routing prefers it only on a separate weekend graph, which the owner chose to build (Weekend graph, below). A road the owner knows is car-free for good while OSM tags it otherwise is corrected by an audited override (Car-free corrections, below).
- Ride time. "We can maybe just use three settings: Weekend, Weekday Rush, Weekday Off-hours?" A plan is for one of the three, defaulting to the setting of the moment in the region's time; rush is Monday to Friday 07:00-10:00 and 16:00-19:00, and a federal holiday counts as the weekend. The traffic volume data is annual average daily traffic, so the stress tiers do not change between rush and off-peak.
- Cargo Bike. "Also another popular option in our city would be cargo bikes. People use cargo bikes in our region quite often. They tend not to be great on hills. Also have a button to differentiate carrying cargo vs carrying children, which have different levels of traffic stress tolerance." Later the same day the owner decided the choice is carrying cargo or "carrying people", to be more inclusive ("Cargo with passengers" since item 241, 2026-10-03, below). See the Cargo row of the Routing model's presets table.
- Night dropped. "Not sure we really need night. If you're going to be riding at night, you should be carrying a light. It's a legal requirement." There is no Night preset; the lit-streets toggle, the phase 6 `use_lit` fork item and the lit-share and darkness reporting elsewhere in this document are unaffected by this entry, which removes only the ride type. The reasoning stays here and nowhere in the interface, which makes no legal statement.
- Bikepacking dropped. "We can also drop bikepacking, not because it's bad, but cycle.travel does a great job for that use case. Also, most of my bikepacking trips go off the map on the first day." There is no Bikepacking preset, and the points-of-interest scoring its row described is not planned. The reasoning stays here; the interface names no other service.
- Beginner and Recovery dropped. Asked "With stress and hilliness sliders, Recovery, Beginner and Trailmaxxing overlap. What should the ride-type list keep?", the owner first asked "Wouldn't recovery be mostly the same as trail maxxing or beginner?" and then chose "Keep Trailmaxxing only (Recommended)" (the option read: "Drop Recovery and Beginner; Trailmaxxing = stress slider at max. Anyone wanting fewer hills moves the hill slider. Shortest list."). The road exposure report and its gap warning, which Beginner's row named, remain a route-summary feature for any ride rather than a preset's.
- Other presets. "Add the other presets too, like trailmaxxing". Every row of the Routing model's presets table but Night, Bikepacking, Beginner and Recovery is offered at layers 1 and 2; what a row gives layer 4 is not built, and its card says what the ride type does today.
- Ride-type dialog. "Have the ability to change route modes in a modal dialog instead of a radio button. Selecting a preset will change the sliders." Choosing a ride type moves both sliders to its starting positions; a slider moved afterwards shows the ride as custom, based on that type, with a way back to the type's own settings.
- Weekend graph. Asked whether weekend rides should prefer weekend car-free roads in routing, the owner chose "Build the weekend graph" (the option read: "Weekend rides actively prefer car-free roads; more memory and rebuild time on this 12 GB machine."). A fourth variant, the standard graph's weekend twin, carries those roads as off-road paths; a weekend ride on the standard graph routes on it. The e-bike and no-trail graphs have no twin, so their weekend rides report the closures in the breakdown only.
- Default at 90. Asked "Keep Default at 75, or 90?", the owner chose "90".
- Mass Ride's traffic slider. Asked whether it should move, the owner chose "Lock at 0 (Recommended)".
- Cargo electric assist. "Cargo bikes with E assist still have problems on hills. They tend to be very heavy. The assist doesn't cancel out the hill in many cases. People with more powerful motors can increase hill tolerance. We want to be more novice friendly." Cargo Bike's electric-assist toggle applies the e-bike graph's legality and a somewhat faster pace and keeps the hill-averse start; a rider with a strong motor moves the hills slider.
- Legal but avoid. Asked about US 340 in West Virginia, the owner chose "Legal but avoid" (the option read: "Leave access as OSM has it; the stress penalty (LTS 4) already pushes Default and calmer rides off it when there's an alternative.") and then typed: "Maybe make a 5th category for legal but to be avoided". Stress tier 5 sits above the Furth scale: the ways the owner curates, and, as an implementation choice the owner has not ruled on, an expressway (`expressway=yes`) posted 50 mph or more. It carries the stress penalty and, on every preset Mass Ride included, a charge each time a route enters it, and it stays routable when it is the only way.
- The owner's curation. "11th street is LTS4, benning road by bridge on road, and south Capitol by bridge on a road are in the avoid category". The 11th Street local span and its south landing are tier 4 (the separate "Steer to the path" penalty they carried was retired the same day; see below); the Benning Road and Frederick Douglass bridge roadways are tier 5, their paths and sidewalks untouched.
- Mass Ride on the Frederick Douglass bridge. "That shouldn't be the case. There were even issues on it on rides that I've done. Any time it's proposed for a mass ride there are strong objections". Tier 5 applies to Mass Ride too; the roadway stays legal.
- Pennsylvania Avenue SE. "Pensylvania turns into an expressway just after the bridge. If you can't take the trail it becomes prohibited to bikes. Probably an LTS4, but useless for mass rides." The Sousa Bridge roadway is tier 4. Asked about the access correction that would have barred bicycles east of the bridge, the owner looked at the road: "Looking at it, there's a highway offramp with a blind corner. It's a level 5 road afterwards." The correction was withdrawn unloaded; Pennsylvania Avenue SE eastbound from the node where the DC 295 off-ramp merges in to the Fairlawn Avenue SE junction is tier 5 and stays legal, and the bridge roadway stays tier 4.
- What tier 5 charges. Asked whether the tier-5 entry charge should share Valhalla's destination-only flag with the 19 mi (31 km) of local-access and private-for-cars ways that already carry it, the owner chose "Only tier-5 roads (Recommended)" (the option read: "Rework it so only 'legal but avoid' roads pay the penalty; local-access streets stay normal."). Tier 5 is marked with a use no other way in the graph has, and the destination-only penalty is back at Valhalla's own value.
- The 11th Street penalty. Asked whether the separate "Steer to the path" penalty should stay now that 11th Street is tier 4, the owner chose "Retire it (Recommended)". The tier-4 stress penalty does the steering; the owner's words are kept with the curated rows and in fixtures/crossings/README.md.
- The curated list. Asked whether to load `fixtures/overrides/2026-09-27-owner-stress.json` on the live deployment, the owner answered "Yes, load it", at the next deploy.
- Why a stretch is adjusted. "we could have something clickable as a link to why we'd consider a particular stretch of road level 5, or also why a particular stretch of road might be adjusted, perhaps hidden. For instance we could deviate from the typical LTS because of road conditions, known aggressive drivers, problematic intersections, etc. Also we could down adjust a road if this is the better route among similar routes." Every curated stress row names its adjustment: a stable id shared by the ways of one stretch, a category (road conditions, driver behaviour, intersection, sightlines, better among alternatives, other), public or hidden, whether the owner has approved its words, and an optional note for riders that describes the road and its traffic and never a neighbourhood or its people. A curated tier may sit below the computed one. The segment table carries the id, and for a public, approved adjustment the computed tier, the direction, the category and the note; a hidden or unapproved one is only "adjusted". The owner's quoted reason stays in the audit trail. The cards that show it come later.
- Where an adjustment's note is shown. Of the proposed notes: "In many cases there's an acceptable trail. Only provide the warnings if the route goes over the road." A note is shown in the summary of a route that rides over the stretch, not on a click on the map, unless a row says otherwise; the six notes are approved as written for that. Asked whether to add a speed category, the owner chose "Yes, add 'speed' (Recommended)", and US 340 is in it.
- Pennsylvania Avenue SE westbound. Asked whether the westbound approach through the DC 295 ramps should also be legal but avoid, the owner chose "Yes, avoid westbound too" (2026-09-28). Westbound from the DC 295 north ramp through the off-ramp merge and the DC 295 south ramp to the Sousa Bridge's east end is tier 5; the bridge roadway stays tier 4. Its note is proposed and waits for the owner's wording.
- Cargo carrying cargo. Asked where carrying cargo should start now that Default is 90, the owner chose "Same as Default, 90 (Recommended)"; Cargo with passengers (then "carrying people"; item 241) stays at 100, which is 80 since the rescale of 2026-10-01.
- Car-free corrections. Asked how to reflect "(most of beach drive in DC is closed to car traffic permanantly)", the owner chose "Our own correction (Recommended)" (the option read: "Allow audited corrections to mark roads car-free (like the access corrections), loaded with your go-ahead."). An access override may write `motor_vehicle=no`; the Beach Drive NW file is prepared and is loaded only with the owner's go-ahead.
- Graded stress. Asked whether LTS 4 should cost more than LTS 3, the owner chose "Yes, grade them (Recommended)" (the option read: "LTS 4 penalised more than LTS 3 at every slider position.") and then typed: "I'd probably want LTS 4 to be twice the stress level of LTS 3 at least." A way's stress level is the cost its tier adds per metre over the same edge with no tier, as a multiple of the time cost; LTS 4's is at least twice LTS 3's at every slider position above 0 on roads posted up to 50 mph, and at least 1.75 times on faster ones, the most the pinned Valhalla's costing allows without a fork (docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Graded stress"). Tier 5 stays above LTS 4. Mass Ride, locked at 0, is not graded.
- The westbound Pennsylvania Avenue SE note, "Ramp traffic to and from DC 295 merges in and crosses the lanes." (intersection, route_only): "Approve as written".
- No hard grade cap (against the "Steepest hill I'll accept" control of User-facing controls). Asked whether to add it, the owner said: "I'd probably have an increasing penalty for steeper hills instead." Then: "In many cases it's not just stepness but steepness and length. 10% can be done for 100m of riding, people would just sprint before it. If continued over kilometers, that becomes a hike-a-bike". The avoid half of the hills slider prices each sustained climb by its grade and its length together, short kicks nearly free (docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Sustained grades").
- Brake-riding descents. "I'd say a descent over about 2-3% might actually want to be penalized. There's a point where it's a fun downhill and a point where you're riding the breaks." Asked where it should start, the owner chose "Depends on ride type" (the option read: "Gentler threshold for Cargo (heavy, braking matters most), higher for others."). Sustained descents past a ride type's threshold cost on the avoid half of the hills slider; gentle ones stay free. The thresholds per ride type are proposed in docs/DEVELOPMENT.md for the owner to confirm.
- The bottom of the traffic slider. "Let's make the current setting a traffic tolerant one and then build a full fastest custom. There should be a warning going at this setting." The slider's low end is "Traffic tolerant", not fastest or direct; at 10 or below the panel and the route summary warn "Traffic tolerant: this route may use busy, fast roads." (not on Mass Ride, whose slider is locked at 0 and says why). A true fastest option is a later custom-costing phase.
- Brake grades, sustained grades and Beach Drive NW (2026-09-28). The brake-grade table: "Approve the table" (Cargo 3%, Mass Ride 4%, Group Ride 5%, Default, Trailmaxxing, Gravel and E-bike 6%, Mountain Goat and Fast never). The alternatives-based weighing of sustained grades: "Ship this, fork later (Recommended)"; a per-edge cost waits for a costing fork. The Beach Drive NW car-free correction: "Yes, load it", at the next deploy.
- The rebuild's time limit. Asked "The map rebuild has a 6-hour time limit ... Raise the limit to 8 hours?", the owner chose "Yes, 8 hours (Recommended)" (2026-09-28). A fourth graph (the weekend twin) adds about 25 minutes to a quiet rebuild and much more to a loaded one, and a six-hour run had already failed on its budget with three. The rebuild's timeout is 8 hours (PLAN.md:58).
- Hills against traffic. Asked whether hill avoidance may make a route busier than the same trip at the middle of the hills slider, the owner chose "Traffic wins (Recommended)" (2026-09-28): never let hill avoidance make a route busier than the middle setting's; keep the calmer route, at the cost of one extra router call per hill-avoiding plan. On the avoid half the same trip is asked for again at the middle and both are traced; if the hill-avoiding route's exposure (LTS 3, twice LTS 4 and three times tier 5 metres) is worse, the middle's route is the answer.

**2026-09-29 to 2026-10-01. Stress calibration (OWNER-DECISIONS 84-146, 176, 177).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 84, of MD 450's wide lane: "I'd probably say that's LTS3. Perhaps reduce it by 1 or so. But more objective criteria could work too."
- 86: "There are some other misses. For some reason 22nd street is LTS4, but it's a perfectly normal road, just with more lanes." 87: "Multi-lane in a city isn t nearly that problematic." 101: "We should change the rules, those are wrong."
- 104: "Also for DC, "Riding a bicycle on sidewalks is strictly illegal within the Central Business District (CBD), which covers downtown D.C. (bounded generally by 23rd St NW, Massachusetts Ave NW, 2nd St NE/SE, and D St/Constitution Ave)." This doesn't apply to the federal areas on the mall and around the capitol that are within that zone. THere shouldn't be sidewalk riding here."
- 106: "There s a difference between multi-lane 1-way roads and 2-way roads." 109: "Conneticut avenue is definitely 3, and possibly 4. However keep in mind that we shouldn't have the same criteria for 2 way traffic vs 1 way. 1 Way is typically lower stress at similar characteristics."
- 107: "Also to note for the Arboretum, the speed limit is 20 MPH, so LTS 3 is way too high." 108: "DC law now has default at 20", and "Also, keep a note that the default speed limit in DC on all streets and highways is 20 mph, or 15 mph in alleys. There might be missing speed limits that assign a much higher LTS." 112: "Use MD and VA defaults there."
- 110, alleys: "Stronger". 111: "We will need a whole dedicated MTB mode. There's a whole set of criteria there." 113: "The capitol grounds are a very good place to ride and avoid some major roads, so definitely include."
- 130, the Capitol drives: "Yes, open them". 131, the Montgomery County parkways: "Set them to 25 mph (Recommended)". 132, divided roads: "Approve". 137, where the states come from: "From our OSM data (Recommended)". 140, quiet multi-lane streets at 35 mph: "Yes." 141: "Make arterials LTS 3 or greater unless there s bike infrastructure. I d put most of the Arterials east of the Anacostia river as avoid." 143, 22nd Street NW's three-lane one-way at LTS 3: "3 is probably a decent level. I wouldn t expect an experienced cycle to have issues, but wouldn t take a novice". 144, asked which arterials east of the Anacostia to strike: struck "11th St SE", "Ridge Rd SE", "River bridges"; everything else (South Capitol St SW included) approved to load as Avoid. 145, whether a paved shoulder on a major road counts as bike infrastructure for the arterial floor: "Yes, count it". 146, whether to re-read the two DC dataset pages for their licence text: "Yes, re-read them". 176, what District collector (tertiary) streets should get, about 51 mi of them LTS 1 under the 20 mph default without counts: "At least LTS 2 (Recommended)". 177, whether the 17 overpasses well away from the river stay on the east-of-the-Anacostia Avoid list: "Yes, keep them in".

  Implemented as (`routemaker.stress` unless named; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md "Calibration of the tiers" has the detail and the deviations from the literature review):
  - Urban lane count: in the urban-area layer a one-way with up to two lanes is read on the single-lane row; a two-way multi-lane street, a one-way of three lanes or more ("wide one-way floor", item 143) and a carriageway of a divided road (`routemaker.divided`, item 132) are LTS 3 at least, LTS 4 from 30 mph over 8,000 vehicles a day. A quiet one keeps its low-volume relief down to LTS 3 (item 140, owner-approved).
  - A decent painted lane at 40 mph is LTS 3, not from three lanes a direction.
  - Missing speed limits take the state's default: DC 20 mph, 15 in alleys; Maryland and Virginia urban areas 25/30/35 by class. The states come from the extract's own admin_level=4 boundaries each rebuild (`pipeline.states`), and the rebuild refuses when the District, Maryland or Virginia is missing.
  - Arterials (trunk, primary, secondary and their links) are LTS 3 at least without a painted, buffered or protected lane, a credited paved shoulder (item 145, owner-approved), or a separately mapped facility beside them (item 141 (a), everywhere). Collectors (tertiary and its links) are LTS 2 at least without such a provision, and a count may push them higher (item 176, everywhere). A rideable shoulder under either floor reads as a lane of its width. At 20 mph and below a count of 1,500-8,000 a day is LTS 2 (Furth v2.2). Item 141 (b): the District's trunk, primary and secondary ways east of the river, less what item 144 struck and the ways the 2026-09-27 file already curates, are Avoid (tier 5, hidden): fixtures/overrides/2026-09-30-owner-arterials-east-of-anacostia.json, 779 rows.
  - Alleys pay the stress penalty, priced like LTS 3 in routing (item 110).
  - No sidewalk riding in DDOT's CBD, the federal areas in it and the Architect of the Capitol's polygon excepted (`routemaker.cbd`).
  - The Capitol drives: an approved access file (fixtures/overrides/2026-09-30-owner-capitol-drives.json) opens 1.15 mi, not the 1.9 mi asked about: 25 service drives (0.99 mi, access=permissive, bicycle=yes, motor_vehicle=no) and four linear pedestrian drives (0.16 mi, bicycle=yes). The thirteen pedestrian areas on the grounds (most of Capitol Driveway NE/SE as mapped) are areas, which the router does not route at all, so no access row can open them.
  - The Montgomery County parkways with no posted speed are classified at 25 mph, from a checked-in speed file read at classify time (`routemaker.speed_corrections`, fixtures/speed/); a posted speed wins.

**2026-09-29 and 2026-09-30. Mountain-bike singletrack (OWNER-DECISIONS 90, 91, 111, 148).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 90: "There s several mountain bike trails too, I wouldn t route bikes on them either unless that s specifically looked for. However, that s a whole different routing engine." 91: "Gravel is different. Most bikes can handle gravel, few would want to route through singletrack. We can have an "avoid gravel check" though." 111, asked whether Gravel and Mountain Goat keep access to singletrack: "We will need a whole dedicated MTB mode. There's a whole set of criteria there." 148: "Mountain bike mode is much later. Most of these are to do, that's a backlog."

  Implemented as: every current ride type avoids singletrack, Gravel and Mountain Goat included (a trail rated 1 or more on mtb:scale or mtb:scale:imba, unless paved: review r1 found Upper Rock Creek, Northwest Branch, Muddy Branch, Gunpowder Falls and the Cross County Trail rated on asphalt); gravel stays routable for all, with an "Avoid gravel" box off by default, which rides the route as a Road bicycle at the preset's own speed, so every surface worse than compacted is priced steeply (Cross admits gravel and Hybrid dirt, which the dial alone left unpriced). **Backlog: a dedicated MTB mode** (item 111; "much later", item 148; Backlog and next release, above) - its own graph or variant that seeks singletrack by `mtb:scale` and the rest of the MTB criteria, which the current ride types close.

**2026-10-01. Agency data (OWNER-DECISIONS 149-162, 179-184).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 149 (2026-09-30), of the Montgomery Planning "Bicycle Level of Traffic Stress" layer: "I think this version does not. They also have an LTS5, which we can mark as avoid."
- 151 (2026-09-30): "Yes, include it and the moco dataset."
- 159 (2026-10-01), citing Baltimore City Code Art. 1 §9-1(h) (https://codes.baltimorecity.gov/us/md/cities/baltimore/code/1/9-1#(h)): "Datasets published on the Open Data Portal shall be available to the public on an open license basis, with no restrictions on copying, publishing, further distributing, modifying, or using the data for any non-commercial or commercial purpose." Credit "City of Baltimore, Open Baltimore".
- 179, 16th Street NW and Connecticut Avenue NW at LTS 4 from the Roadway Block's lanes and counts: "Keep LTS 4 (Recommended)".
- 180, the Montgomery gap (residential streets we rate LTS 2 and Montgomery LTS 1): "Backlog with decimal model (Recommended)".
- 181, Montgomery's LTS 5 as Avoid: "Load it (Recommended)", with the Montgomery County Planning credit in the same change.
- 182, the Baltimore facilities rows: "Load Baltimore facilities".
- 183, Alexandria's posted 20 mph: "Apply Alexandria 20 mph", sourced from the CC0 or OSM side, never from the internal-only Transport Streets data.
- 184, Baltimore's centerline speed against OSM's posted maxspeed: "Fill gaps only (Recommended)".

  Implemented as (docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Agency street layers"; fixtures/datasets/README.md has the licence records):
  - Sources (149, 151, 159; the reconciliation item 162 asked for). The ingest procedure above now lists DC's Roadway Block (CC BY 4.0), Montgomery Planning (open with attribution, cleared through the three questions on this date, fixtures/datasets/README.md), Open Baltimore (Baltimore City Code Art. 1 §9-1(h), recorded as the licence basis) and Alexandria's Bike Lane Routes (CC0) as cleared, and Arlington's Bike Comfort Index and Alexandria's Transport Streets as internal comparison only (item 155). The installer and the analysis script refuse a layer under `internal-only/`, the internal reports are refused inside the repository, and `reports/**/internal/` is ignored.
  - The Roadway Block and Baltimore's centerline are conflated onto OSM ways at classification (`routemaker.agency_roads`, `pipeline.conflation.road_facts_by_way`): DC's posted speed, lanes by direction, one-way, bike lane and parking take precedence over OSM's tags; Baltimore's speed fills only where OSM has no `maxspeed` (184). A bike facility OSM maps as its own way is never written onto the road, an agency one-way never undoes OSM tagging that says two-way (in the District superseded by item 190, 2026-10-02, below), a slip road keeps its own lanes, and each carriageway takes its own direction's lanes and lane. The Roadway Block's count fills only where no count layer reached a way.
  - 179: no change; the lane totals include bus lanes and reversible lanes are assumed to operate (both noted in reports/data-comparison/before-after.md).
  - 180: no change in this rebuild; the gap is an input to FOLLOWUP-DECIMAL-STRESS (reports/data-comparison/moco-lts-comparison.md).
  - 181: fixtures/overrides/2026-10-01-owner-moco-lts5-avoid.json, 409 ways, 44.6 mi, tier 5 (Avoid), hidden, one adjustment per street. Credit "Roads to avoid in Montgomery County: Bicycle Level of Traffic Stress, Montgomery County Planning Department" in `routing.ATTRIBUTION`, the map credits and docs/OPERATIONS.md, in the same change.
  - 182: fixtures/overrides/2026-10-01-owner-baltimore-facilities.json, 256 stress rows (261 in round 1; held to the 264 the owner approved, 2026-10-02 below; the tier with the city's recorded lane or track tagged) and 180 `bicycle=designated` access rows; OSM edits upstream remain the durable fix.
  - 183: not implemented. A jurisdiction default for Alexandria's residential streets would need a public basis, and none is in the repository, its documents or the stored item metadata: the CC0 Bike Lane Routes layer carries no speeds, and OSM, in a box around the city, tags 376 residential, unclassified and living-street ways at 25 mph (many surveyed) against 18 at 20 mph. The 20 mph figure came only from the internal Transport Streets layer, which item 183 rules out as a source. What is needed is the city's ordinance or policy setting 20 mph on residential streets (its URL or text from the owner, as item 159 supplied Baltimore's code), after which it is a default like the District's 20 mph, read for ways inside the city's boundary from the extract.
  - The bike-lane reach beside parking is Furth's 15 ft (Mekuria, Furth and Nixon 2012, Table 2), not 13.5 ft, and the parking width is added only where DC records the lane beside parking.

**2026-10-01. The traffic slider's top end, and intersections (OWNER-DECISIONS 157, 163 to 175, 185 to 188).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded. What was built is in the Routing model, above ("The traffic slider's top end, intersection costs and junction markers"), and the Mass Ride section under Phases.

- 163: "Another note that I see here when remaking the app. I'd actually like to see the slider for traffic stress to have a lot more available penalty for higher LTS roads. There are times that I want to go for a ride and I'm willing to add 10 or 20 miles to my trip just so it's more relaxing. Clearly there's times where you'd be willing to handle more stressful roads for a shorter trip, but for a relaxing weekend ride where the point is in many cases to do miles, a higher distance tradeoff could make sense." 164: "The 10-20 miles is just an example, but the upper end could be much greater than straight line distance, just warn people."
- 165: "Also, note that crossing a busy road should get some level of penalty, especially in rural areas where it's usually a stop sign against free-flowing traffic. Much lower penalty for traffic signals, and no penalty for grade-separated crossings." 166: "Make it less of a penalty for right turns and more of one for left. For most roads, a right turn might be close to 0." 167: "Also, having to cross several lanes to get into the left turn can add stress too, though box turns are an option."
- 169: "Another thing to note with rural roads, if you re on the free-flowing route of traffic and there s a stoplight or sign for cross traffic, the penalty is much less than being on the cross side. Sliplanes should get a penalty too."
- 171: "Let's build similar intersection penalties in mass rides too with left turns and the like. Don't overpenalize neighborhood roads with stop signs though. They rarely cause issue. Also DC law lets bikes roll through if safe." (DC's roll-through rule is the owner's account; not legal advice.) 172: "Also, when building a regular ride map, put markers on intersections in a route, such as an orange marker for higher stress intersections and a red one for very high stress intersections. That way people know where to watch out or reroute."
- The review's questions, answered in chat (185 to 188): 185 "Fix causes first (Recommended)": divided-road crossings count once with a median-refuge credit, trail crossings whose signal is not mapped cap at orange labelled "signal not mapped", re-measure, keep the thresholds for now. 186 "Cap at box turn (Recommended)": at signals a left never costs more than the two-stage box turn (about 500 ft). 187: "Make max calm even higher. Let's see what 150 would do. Trailmaxxing is about relaxation, not commuting." 188 "Keep 2% + 164 ft (Recommended)".
- Mass Ride, the same day: 157 (surface condition penalties, DC only), 173 (the owner's measured analysis of how tightly three DC Bike Party rides pack, `reports/owner/cyclist_packing_density.md`), 174: "That speed was along a turn, to slow people down. 6-8 is more realistic", and 175: "The intent was to count the cyclists, this was a reuse." The Mass Ride section's flow model, surface bullet and intersection bullet follow them.

**2026-10-02. DC precedence (OWNER-DECISIONS 189-191; 216 and 217, below, amend 190).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 189, Irving Street NW/NE at LTS 1 from DC's protected-lane record: "Irving street has a protected bike lane with some nice concrete barriers".
- 190: "DC data takes priority over OSM. It's updated regularly". Recorded with it: within DC, Roadway Block values (speed, lanes, one-way, bike facility, including a record of no facility) override OSM tags; the exception kept for modelling is that where OSM draws the facility as its own way (`cycleway=separate`), the facility stays on that way rather than being copied onto the road. Baltimore keeps item 184 (fill gaps only).
- 191: "DC roads and especially bike infrastructure changes quite frequently, so it's likely OSM data is stale. However, report the discrepancies when you see them."

  Implemented as (docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Agency street layers", Precedence; `routemaker.agency_roads.overlay`, wired once in `pipeline.conflation.overlay_road_facts`). The rule is applied as review r2 scoped it, because a Roadway Block describes the whole road and not each of the OSM ways that make it up; each override is named per way (`Overlay.precedence`), each exception is counted with its reason, and reports/data-comparison/before-after.md measures each row on its own:
  - A, DC records no facility: OSM's painted lane is removed, decided per block - only where no block along the way records a facility, and a direction one block records keeps OSM's lane on that side. 87 ways, 4.8 mi; 45 change tier (21 lower, 24 higher). A track OSM maps on the road is kept and reported.
  - B, DC one-way over OSM's explicit two-way: followed, except on reversible-lane blocks (Clara Barton Parkway), junction stubs of 100 ft (30 m) or less, and where the direction is unknown. 17 ways, 1.1 mi; 2 change tier (18 and 3 before review r3 made the stub threshold inclusive).
  - Rows B and C4 and the filled one-ways set the routing direction too since item 216 (below); before it they reached the classifier only.
  - C4, DC two-way over OSM one-way on a plain street: followed. 207 ways, 15.4 mi; 9 change tier, all to higher stress (208 and 10 before review r3). Not followed (C1-C3): a divided road's carriageways, a pair of opposite one-way ways on one block, a one-way side lane beside a busier two-way main carriageway (review r3), slip roads, motorways and trunk roads, and also roundabouts, junction stubs, unnamed ways and ways running on onto a one-way block.
  - D, slip roads keep their own lanes and take no block count; E, a facility OSM maps as its own way is never written onto the road - now also on any other way matched to the same block (Irving Street's motor carriageway, `bicycle=no`, beside the carriageway OSM marks separate) - and a protected lane is never written where OSM says `bicycle=no`, `bicycle=use_sidepath` or `cycleway*=no`.
  - With review r2's other fixes: a lane is a contraflow lane only where DC flags `BIKELANE_CONTRAFLOW` (a lone lane on an unflagged one-way block is the with-flow lane); a two-way way on a one-way block keeps OSM's lanes rather than the busier direction's count both ways; a freeway-class way takes a differently named block only where the block is itself a freeway (Canal Road NW no longer takes M Street NW's).
  - 189: Irving Street's protected lane is on the separate ways OSM maps for it; the motor carriageways sharing those blocks keep OSM's tags and their own tier (LTS 3-4), so the rider is routed on the protected lane, not the road.
  - 191: every rebuild writes the DC-against-OSM discrepancy report, `<DATA_ROOT>/rebuild/reports/dc-osm-discrepancies.md` and `.csv` (`pipeline.discrepancies`, from the overlay the rebuild classified with; docs/OPERATIONS.md, "After a rebuild"), and reports/data-comparison/ keeps a copy written from `dcbal.tsv` (`scripts/analysis/dc_osm_discrepancies.py`). It lists every District way where the two disagree, by type (facility present or absent, facility kind, contraflow, one-way, lanes, speed), with the OSM and DC values, the tier effect, and whether the District's value was applied; the not-applied items are summarised by reason, the owner's overrides listed in full, and every item is in the CSV. Nothing in it is imported into OSM: the Roadway Block is CC BY 4.0 and an import into ODbL would need a licence waiver.
  - The override files keep within the rows the owner approved in items 181 and 182 (reports/data-comparison/owner-approved-override-ways.json): MoCo 409 of 411 ways; Baltimore 256 of 264 stress rows and 180 access rows, the differences listed in the two reports for the owner.

**2026-10-02. Contraflow lanes (OWNER-DECISIONS 192, 193; 216 and 219, below, amend them).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 192: "contraflow lanes are not for group rides or mass rides, many routing engines put people on this when it's not appropriate."
- 193, on 192's Group Ride with trails on: "with trails on that's fine".

  Implemented as (docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Contraflow on the no-trail graph"; `pipeline.variants.close_contraflow`, applied in `inject` to the no-trail variant only): on a way that is one-way for motor traffic the no-trail graph, which is Mass Ride's graph and any ride's with trails off, has bicycles follow the one-way. The standard, weekend and e-bike graphs are unchanged, so a Group Ride with trails on may use contraflow lanes as 193 allows, and there is no trace-and-exclude step. Group Ride's trails-off toggle is not offered yet; when it ships it takes this graph and so inherits the closure. The closure is a routing rule only: the stress tier, the facility class, the tiles and every map layer are computed once from the way's own tags and are unchanged. In the 2026-09-25 region extract it closes 192 one-way ways, 16.0 miles (25.7 km) (187 ways, 15.9 miles, named by a contraflow tag; 5 ways, 0.1 miles, read by the router as two-way because they have a lane on both sides). Found by the review and fixed separately, on every graph: the remap's conditional-access resolution had opened 2 ways, 2.9 miles, of Pulaski Highway, a one-way trunk barred to bicycles except at weekends, in both directions. That was not a contraflow lane, and 193 does not cover it, so the remap now never opens a one-way against its traffic from a conditional unless the way itself grants contraflow; no other way in the extract was affected. It takes effect with the next rebuild, which is the combined one.

**2026-10-02. The round-2 review's answers (OWNER-DECISIONS 194 to 196).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 194, on item 187 (answering a question that offered "Put Trailmaxxing at 100 now, and add a backlog task to generate routes that seek out trails and protected lanes"): "Build trail-seeking search". Trailmaxxing moves to 100 and Cargo with passengers (then "carrying people"; item 241) stays at 80.
- 195, slip lanes: "Flag only when you cross it (Recommended)". Orange only when the route crosses the slip lane's path, no marker when the rider rides straight past it along the road.
- 196, the median-refuge credit: "x0.75 (Recommended)".

  Implemented as (docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Intersection costs, the calm search and the detour warning"): Trailmaxxing starts at 100, where the calm search runs at its top rate; a slip lane is crossed where its arms lie on both sides of the rider's path, on the right of a left turn, or leave the road on the right of a rider going straight in a painted or separated bike lane (the right hook; an implementation reading, for the owner); the refuge credit is x0.75 for two crossings of one divided road's carriageways only. **Backlog: FOLLOWUP-TRAIL-SEEK** - a candidate generator for the calm search that seeks out the trails and protected lanes near the straight line (via points on them, from the segment table), rather than only excluding busy stretches of the router's own routes; it is what would make a calm setting above 100 meaningful (item 187's measurements: past a rate of about 10 the search chooses among the same nested few routes). Until it exists the slider tops out at 100 (rate 10) and the wider search (`WIDE_SEARCH_FROM_RATE`) stays off. (Superseded: FOLLOWUP-TRAIL-SEEK is built, items 201 to 203, below. The slider still tops out at 100 and the wider search is still off, now because the seek's measurements found that positions above 100 plan what 100 does.)

**2026-10-02. DC records the owner overrode, and review r3's follow-ups (OWNER-DECISIONS 197, 198).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 197: DC records of 20 mph on Canal Rd NW (block dc-4633425-0) and the Whitehurst Fwy (dc-4636053-0), owner 2026-10-02: "Override: keep LTS 4 (Recommended)". A stress override holds them at the OSM speeds and LTS 4, and they stay listed in the discrepancy report.
- 198: N Charles St, Baltimore (970453181), owner 2026-10-02: "LTS 2 as derived (Recommended)".

  Implemented as:
  - 197: fixtures/overrides/2026-10-02-owner-canal-whitehurst.json withholds the two blocks' posted speed (`agency_blocks`, `routemaker.agency_roads.withheld_blocks`; fixtures/overrides/README.md) rather than holding the tier with stress rows, so OSM's 35 mph is what the classifier reads and LTS 4 follows from it, and OSM's way ids, which change when a way is split, are not what holds it. The blocks are named by DC's BLOCKKEY with their ROUTENAME as a check (`agency_roads.resolve_withheld`), not by the installed ids quoted above: those are built from OBJECTID, the ArcGIS row number, which DC's republishing may reassign (gate review, should-fix 1); a correction that finds no installed block, or another street, withholds nothing and the rebuild's log warns of it. Canal Road NW's five trunk ways (0.38 mi) are LTS 4 again. The 17 ways with an OSM speed are listed in the discrepancy report as not applied, "owner override".
  - 198: N Charles Street 970453181 keeps its derived LTS 2 row in the Baltimore file (fixtures/overrides/README.md).
  - Review r3's named follow-ups: a DC-flagged contraflow lane on a block with no recorded traffic direction runs against OSM's one-way (Argonne Place, Champlain Street, T Street and 8th Street NW, 8 ways); C4 leaves a one-way side lane beside a busier two-way main carriageway on its block one-way, with OSM's lanes (K Street NW's service lanes, 41 ways after the gate review: a roundabout is not a two-way main road, and a one-way carrying the main road on from its end node is not beside it, so Cedar Avenue 555136043 is LTS 2 again); the junction-stub threshold is 100 ft (30 m) or less, to the whole metre (New Jersey Avenue NW 1508260473, 30.2 m); `highway=proposed` ways take no block; the discrepancy report is written by every rebuild (191 above).

**2026-10-02. The round-3 answers (OWNER-DECISIONS 199, 200).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 199, right hook at a slip lane: "Yes, flag right hooks (Recommended)". Recorded with it: riding straight in a bike lane while a right-turn channel peels off across it counts as crossing the slip lane (item 195).
- 200, approach-signal reach: "100 ft (Recommended)" (30 m).

  Implemented as: the right-hook reading of item 195 (round 2, above) stays, and the approach walk for a junction's signal reaches 100 ft (30 m) up each approach (Routing model, round 2 and round 3).

**2026-10-02. FOLLOWUP-TRAIL-SEEK (OWNER-DECISIONS 201 to 203; 187, 2026-10-01, and 194, round 2, above).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 201, FOLLOWUP-TRAIL-SEEK timing (the backlog item of 194, round 2 above, after 187 of 2026-10-01): "Before the rebuild". The trail-seeking candidate search is built and reviewed before the combined rebuild.

  **Implemented as** (201; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "The trail seek"): `core.trailseek`, run after the exclusion rounds from a calm rate of 10 (position 100, the top). It reads the traffic-free paths and trails, the protected facilities and the car-free roads at LTS 1 or 2 from the segment table in a band around the straight line and the router's best route, and finds the runs of that network that leave the route and rejoin it later where what the replaced stretch costs in the search's own terms (the calm rate times its LTS 3, 4 and Avoid exposure) most exceeds the detour. The best corridor, the best two in order, and the best different trail are asked of the router as `through` locations; each route is scored and guarded exactly like every other candidate (junction costs, climb, Traffic wins at 2% + 164 ft [50 m]). Measured on twelve trips the seek changes two routes (a little calmer, a little longer), and positions above 100 (rates of 447 and 19,027, the "125" and "150" of item 187) plan exactly the routes 100 does, so the slider is not extended: its top stays 100 and Trailmaxxing stays at 100 (both the owner's call; the numbers are in the DEVELOPMENT section). Not built then (superseded: the trail credit of item 202, next, is that score for Trailmaxxing): a score for trail miles as such (a quiet street and a trail with equal exposure score equally here; the router's own costing already prefers the trail), which is what a setting above 100 would need to mean something different from 100.

- 202, trail credit: "Trail bonus for Trailmaxxing only (Recommended)". Only the Trailmaxxing preset rewards each mile of trail, so its routes go out of their way to ride trails. Default and the other presets are unchanged. (Superseded by 257, 2026-10-03: "Drop it". The credit is gone.)
- 203, trail seek with via points: "Now, before rebuild". The seek runs leg by leg on plans with stops.

  **Implemented as** (202, 203; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "The trail credit and the seek leg by leg"): a preset dial, `Preset.trail_credit` (0.5 on Trailmaxxing, 0 elsewhere, not a slider value and not in the link: the link carries the ride type and the slider, and the API works the credit out), worth that many metres of quiet riding per metre of trail in the search's score. Trail is the seek's own predicate: a path or protected way, or a road closed to cars at the ride time, at LTS 1 or 2. Moving the Traffic slider down from 100 fades the credit in step with the calm rate (a fifth of it at 90, none at 80 or below, where there is no calm search). The seek's corridor score counts the credit (trail the corridor adds less trail it replaces), so a route with nothing busy on it still finds a trail; the Traffic-wins guard and the detour warning are unchanged, so the credit never buys more LTS 3 or 4. On plans with stops the seek runs once per leg, within the same 6 s: each leg takes a share of what is left by its straight-line span (at least 2.5 s), is asked for alone, is guarded against its own first exposure, and is spliced into the trip. Measured on the twelve trips the credit changes three routes (more trail, a little longer); 0.25 changes two and 0.75 plans what 0.5 does.
  - 203, the combined correctness review's SF1 (FIX-BACKEND): each leg's own route is read on an allowance of its own (`refine.SEEK_LEG_READ_S`, 2 s), which extends the seek's 6 s budget but never past the answer's trace reserve; the leg's share is worked out after the reading. One leg's "time" no longer ends the seek while the budget has room; an unreadable table still ends it. Live probe, Bethesda - Silver Spring - College Park at Trailmaxxing 100, three runs: the seek ran on both legs every time (corridors 1, asked 1, `limited` none), where 8c96707 gave `limited: time` with nothing asked, 6 of 6.

**2026-10-02. Preset links (OWNER-DECISIONS 204).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 204: "is there a way to start at a given setting for instance routemaker.cieply.com/trailmaxxing would start at the trailmax setting", then "yes" to the proposal: a Caddy redirect from /<preset-id> to /#preset=<preset-id> for exactly the preset ids, with a parity test against the front-end presets.

  Implemented as: the Caddyfile redirects exactly the nine ride-type ids (default, trailmaxxing, group-ride, mass-ride, mountain-goat, gravel, fast, cargo, ebike), each with and without a trailing slash and in any letter case, to the lowercase `/#preset=<id>` with a 302 (not a 308, which a browser keeps with no way for the site to take it back if an id is renamed). The planner already opens a link with only `preset=` on that ride type with its own starting dials. No id collides with a route in the Caddyfile or the Django URLconf. tests/test_preset_links.py holds the redirect list equal to frontend/src/lib/presets.ts and core/presets.py, and runs the file in the `caddy:2.8-alpine` image compose names (a tag, not a digest) where the image is present.

**2026-10-02. The accessibility switch and the colour-blind-friendly palette (OWNER-DECISIONS 208, 209, 211, 212; FOLLOWUP-CVD-PALETTE).** The record's entries are quoted whole, as recorded (each with the recorder's reading), then what was implemented.

- 208: "Colour-vision palette, owner 2026-10-02. Asked to choose between (1) "A colour-blind-friendly palette as an option", (2) "Strengthen the default" and (3) "Both", the owner answered: "1". A setting in the map panel switches the stress colours (map overlay, route line, legend, stress bar) to a palette that stays distinct under protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia. It is remembered per browser, and the default blended palette is unchanged. Front-end only; it goes into the combined branch. (Recorded late: the first append was lost to a shell redirect error on 2026-10-02.)"
- 209: "Contrast settings, owner 2026-10-02: "Sounds good". Under prefers-contrast: more the stress palette starts on the colour-blind-friendly one (a stored choice or ?palette= still wins), with stronger casings, panel borders and muted text. Under forced-colors: active, legend and stress-bar swatches keep their true colours (forced-color-adjust: none, with a system-colour border), markers keep their shape and words, and the app's panels follow system colours. Folded into FOLLOWUP-CVD-PALETTE."
- 211: "Owner 2026-10-02, amending 208/209: "We don't need to start with color blind friendly, but make accessibility a switch." Read as: prefers-contrast never changes the palette on its own. One Accessibility switch in the map panel, off by default and remembered per browser, turns on the colour-blind-friendly palette with stronger line edges, borders and text. ?palette= still overrides the palette. The forced-colors fixes stay automatic, because they repair broken swatches rather than set a preference."
- 212: "Owner 2026-10-02, clarifying 211: "Oh, more contrast causes that. Yes, that's fine. I just don't want it the general default". The Accessibility switch starts on only for browsers reporting prefers-contrast: more; everyone else starts off. A stored choice always wins, and ?palette= still overrides the palette."

  Implemented as (front end only; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "The accessibility switch and the colour-blind-friendly palette", has the measurements and how to re-run them):
  - One "Accessibility" switch (a button with `role="switch"` and `aria-checked`, a one-line description under it) at the top of the panel's Traffic stress section, shown whether or not the stress overlay is available, since the route line, legend and bar follow it too. Off unless the rider turns it on, or the browser reports `prefers-contrast: more` and the rider has not chosen. Precedence for the switch: the stored choice (`localStorage` `routemaker.accessibility`, "on" or "off", read and written in try/catch, falling back to off), then `prefers-contrast: more`, then off. A flip is a choice even where storage refuses it, and lasts the visit. The media query is followed live: with no choice made, turning the system setting on or off with the page open moves the switch, with no reload. The page's stylesheet does not read `prefers-contrast`; the switch is the only path.
  - Precedence for the palette: `?palette=` in the link, then the switch (the `cvd` palette), then the default `blended`, which this item left unchanged (item 274, FOLLOWUP-STRESS-SALIENCE, later changed its LTS 4 to #c80018 and its Avoid to #14040a over a #ee3b2c casing). An address that names a palette still gets the switch's stronger lines, casings, borders and text, and the panel says so.
  - On: the `cvd` palette (LTS 1 #d2eafc, LTS 2 #5d99d2, LTS 3 #cd4b0a, LTS 4 #6a0a06, Avoid #08081e), chosen by measurement so that every neighbouring pair is at least CIEDE2000 20 apart under normal vision and under simulated protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia (Machado 2009, severity 1.0), lightness falls tier by tier, and every tier is at least 3:1 from every base-map surface and both panel themes; lines half a pixel wider; casings black or white and 3 px wider than the line instead of 2 (and the bike-facility rails outside them follow) - since the salience revision (OWNER-DECISIONS 292) only the calm tiers' casings go black or white, a busy tier's (LTS 3 up, what its dash gaps show) is left as it is, and the painted-lane rail widens from 1 px to 1.5 px; `--border` and `--text-muted` strengthened by a class (`a11y`) on the page's root, in both themes, with the legend and stress-bar swatches' borders in the text colour.
  - The switch repaints without a reload: the overlay's lines, casings and facility rails by `setPaintProperty` (`setStressPalette`), the route's coloured sections by cutting the route again (`setRouteSections`, since their colours travel in the GeoJSON), and the legend, the route-colour legend and the stress bar by a React hook (`useStressStyle`) on a style key. Nothing keeps the tiers from import time (`STRESS_TIERS` and `ROUTE_CLASSES` are gone for `currentTiers()` and `routeClasses()`), and a test scans the source for it.
  - The route line in the `cvd` palette: its sections sit on a dark slate casing (#344c4c) instead of the route's blue, which that palette's mid-blue LTS 2 matched (2.22:1, about 20 CIEDE2000 apart; review r1, accessibility). LTS 2 is 3.04:1 and at least 32 apart from it under every vision; no one colour is 3:1 from both the near-white LTS 1 and the near-black Avoid, so the rest are held apart by hue (at least 19 CIEDE2000 under every vision, the best a search found). "Not rated" is a warm grey (#9f9c93) in that palette, at least 24 from every tier and the traffic-free violet, where the default grey was 16 from LTS 2. The default palette's route and grey are unchanged.
  - Forced colours (`forced-colors: active`) is automatic and not a preference: the legend and stress-bar swatches and the stress-bar's segments are `forced-color-adjust: none` with a `CanvasText` border, so they still match the map's own colours; the junction markers keep their shape and the count its words; the panels, buttons and focus rings follow the system's colours (buttons and the switch keep a `ButtonText` border, focus a `Highlight` ring). The intersection markers' shapes are untouched.

**2026-10-02. DC one-way records in every routing graph, and the precedence exceptions (OWNER-DECISIONS 216, 217; amending 190).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 216, DC one-way records and routing direction: "Enforce on all maps (Recommended)". Recorded with it: DC's one-way record is written into every routing graph's tags, with the same exceptions (divided carriageways, pairs, ramps and freeways, junction stubs of 100 ft or less, reversible lanes, separately mapped tracks), and the discrepancy report catches DC errors. About 149 DC ways (9.6 mi).
- 217, item 190's precedence exceptions: "Approve them (Recommended)".

  Implemented as (FIX-BACKEND; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Agency street layers"):
  - 190 as amended: unchanged for speed, lanes, bike facility and parking, which reach the classifier only. The one-way rows (B, C4) and the one-ways the record fills where OSM tags none now also set the routing direction. Row B's exceptions (reversible lanes, a junction stub of 100 ft (30 m) or less, direction not known) now apply to the filled one-ways as well, so the record never writes a guessed direction: 44 DC ways (2.8 mi) "direction unknown" and 23 junction stubs (0.2 mi) that the integrate-2 build (8c96707) filled are now left as OSM has them. Row C1 is read wider for row C4 (below).
  - 216: `agency_roads.overlay` returns `Overlay.routing`, the direction it decided: `oneway` from row B or a fill (`yes`, or `-1` against the way's digitising direction), `oneway=no` from row C4 (rewritten, never removed, because the extract writer lays changes over the source's tags), and, on a one-way the District made with its flagged contraflow lane, `oneway:bicycle=no` and the `opposite_lane`/`opposite_track`. `pipeline.variants.agency_routing_tags` turns that into the keys it changes in the way's OSM tags. Where the record makes a way one-way without a contraflow lane, OSM's two-way tagging that upstream would open the reverse with (a lane each side, `opposite*`, a reverse grant) is closed on every graph, as `close_contraflow` closes it.
  - `run.inject_tags` lays these over the way's tags on all four variants (standard, no-trail, weekend, e-bike) before `variants.inject`, so the no-trail closure still runs last (items 192, 219): a DC contraflow lane is ridden on the standard graph and closed on the no-trail graph. Where an approved access override set one of the same keys, the row stands and the record sets only `oneway` (a closure's `oneway:bicycle=yes` beside an approved `bicycle:backward=yes` made upstream close the with-flow direction).
  - Row C4 is guarded by `divided.carriageway_pairs`: opposite one-ways of one street, the name read without the District's quadrant, within 330 ft (100 m), residential included. The combined correctness review's ways (South Capitol St SW 910656491, 910656606, 1122669898; E St NW 6056366; H St NW 50511181) stay one-way. The item 109 stress flag (150 ft (45 m), names as mapped) is unchanged.
  - The classifier's `oneway`, the segment table's `road_oneway`/`road_lanes`, and the graph now come from one decision. On the before-after's ways, 8c96707 had 341 DC ways (24.6 mi) and 272 Baltimore ways (24.9 mi) where the classifier's one-way differed from the graph's; now none on matched ways do. The roundabouts no block reached, which the classifier read two-way while the graph implies one-way, are item 228's (2026-10-03, below).
  - Routing-direction changes against 8c96707 (DC; Baltimore none): one-way to two-way by row C4, 181 ways (13.8 mi), 21 of them (0.9 mi) LTS 3 or worse; two-way to one-way by row B, 17 ways (1.1 mi), 4 (0.1 mi) LTS 3 or worse; two-way to one-way where the record fills a direction OSM tags none, 50 ways (5.1 mi), 2 (0.1 mi) LTS 3 or worse. Longest: C4 Ellipse Rd NW 50792782, Springhouse Rd 120907202, Park Rd NW 6061744; row B Bryant St NW 583642326, Anacostia Dr SE 50495382, B St SE 581829296; filled Ohio Dr SW 130927049, Crittenden St NE 6053624, Buchanan St NE 77449775. The owner's "about 149 DC ways (9.6 mi)" for B plus filled is 67 ways (6.2 mi) after the direction-known and stub exceptions.
  - The discrepancy report says an applied one-way record is also the routing direction.
  - 217: as built in 8c96707, now also governing the routing direction (216). The divided-carriageway exception is read wider for row C4 (above).
  - Also under 190 (the combined spec review's SF2, Furth's 15 ft reach): the overlay writes a bike lane's width to the millimetre (5 ft is 1.524 m), and the classifier meets a width criterion within `stress.WIDTH_TOLERANCE_M` (half a centimetre). Re-measured: 12 DC ways (0.39 mi) move from LTS 2 to LTS 1 (Fort Dr NW 6051510, 1111087040-42, 1160587233; 40th St NW 6060413, 1058921909, 1058922341, 1111087043, 1160587234; L St NW 130802361; 15th St NE 331880440).
