**2026-10-02. Screen-reader users and the route description (OWNER-DECISIONS 220; part of 215).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 220, screen-reader users: "There are actually a decent amount of blind cyclists, especially in this area, so screen reader accessibility might make sense. Most workout as stokers in tandems." Asked how far to go before the rebuild, the owner answered "Also a text route description". Recorded with it: this round fixes every screen-reader finding of the combined a11y review (live announcement of detour tiers and very-high-stress junctions, "Higher stress" wording, a skip link past the map markers, no double-read titles, the slider's aria-describedby, junction card focus) and adds a readable stretch-by-stretch route description; it pulls the stress-by-position part of item 215 forward.

  Implemented as, the accessibility fixes (FIX-A11Y, from reports/COMBINED-review-a11y.md; every screen-reader finding was treated as must-fix):
  - 2.4.11: on a map 640 px wide or less (MapLibre's own compact width) the credits start as MapLibre's collapsed "i", with a short "© OpenStreetMap" beside it as CSS text that screen readers skip; the full credits are one tap away, and opened on a phone they scroll (max-height 22vh). The attribution control is added by hand before the scales, so the scales clear the zoom buttons at 320 and 375 px, collapsed or open.
  - 2.4.3 and 4.1.2, the junction card: opened from the list it leaves the focus on the row, and the junction is centred in the part of the map the sheet does not cover; opened from a marker it focuses its close button and, closed by the button or Escape, gives the focus back to that junction's marker, even after a redraw. Escape closes it when the focus is in it, on a marker or row, on the canvas or on the page, and keeps its own meaning anywhere else (the search box, the ride-type dialog). The card is `role="dialog"`, named like "Very high stress junction, at 1.9 mi (3.0 km)", its close button "Close junction card". After a group's zoom the focus goes to the first member's new marker, and a focused marker keeps the focus through a redraw (`lib/junctionCard.ts`).
  - 1.4.11 and 2.4.7: one map focus ring, #111827 with a 2 px white inner ring in both themes, on the junction markers, MapLibre's markers and buttons, the credits links and the "i", inset on the canvas; in forced colours a CanvasText ring around a Canvas inner ring on the markers, and a 3 px `Highlight` outline on MapLibre's zoom and compass buttons.
  - 4.1.3: the route announcement adds the detour tier ("Strong warning: 2.4 times the direct distance, 4.1 mi (6.5 km) longer."; "times", not "×", which is read as "multiplication sign"), the straight-line notice's first sentence, and "N very high stress junctions." "Planning..." is shown but outside the live region, and the route sentence enters it only after standing for 700 ms (`lib/settle.ts`, `SettledText`), so one release is one announcement. Slider keys commit after 600 ms of rest, pointer-up and blur at once: four arrow presses are one plan and one announcement.
  - 4.1.2, 1.3.1 and 3.1.5, the slider: its name is its label alone ("Traffic") and its words are its value text; the calm note (and the Hills notes) are its `aria-describedby`, in sentences of 25 words or fewer.
  - 2.4.6 and 1.4.10: list rows say "Higher stress" and "Very high stress", with hidden separators, so a row is heard as "Higher stress, At 1.2 mi (2.0 km): Left turn …"; at 320 px the reason wraps onto the row's second line. (Since FINAL-FIX each row, group and member is named by one `aria-label`, "Higher stress, At 1.2 mi (2.0 km): ...", as the grid put a space before each hidden separator.)
  - 2.4.1: a skip link, "Skip to the route planner", is the first stop; it focuses `#route-planner` and leaves the address's fragment alone, because the plan lives there (`lib/skipLink.ts`). The markers keep their tab stops: sighted keyboard riders use them, the card's focus return needs them, and the list is a screen reader's full equivalent.
  - 2.5.8 and 4.1.2: markers whose centres are within 24 px group at every zoom but the last (18); a group click fits to the map's maximum zoom; the focused or hovered marker sits on top. The junction markers have no title attribute, so nothing is read twice.
  - Plain language: "Color-blind friendly stress colors", and the route legend is "Route color legend".
  - Checked by `scripts/a11y/run.sh` (Vite in node:22 and Chromium from the Playwright image, both by digest, offline, one browser: 53 checks) and `scripts/mutants_a11y.py` (45 of 45 mutants killed).
  - Left for later: the calm-search note is not announced; in forced colours the canvas ring takes the system's outline colour; opened credits can still cover a card on a phone.

  Implemented as, the route description (ROUTE-DESCRIPTION; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "The route description (item 220)"; docs/OPERATIONS.md, "The route description needs the street names in the trace"):
  - `routemaker.describe`, a pure module, builds it from what the plan already has: the trace's pieces (now carrying edge names, use and headings), each piece's tier and facility, and the junction events. The only new router input is the `edge.names` attribute on the `trace_attributes` call the plan already makes; route, trace and locate counts and the routes are unchanged. `POST /api/route` returns it as `description`.
  - Entries are stretches, junctions (a flagged junction that is not a street change) and stops. A turn belongs to the stretch it enters, so each entry is one sentence: "0.5 to 7.5 mi (0.8 to 12.1 km): Right onto Capital Crescent Trail, traffic-free path." A range is written with "to", not a dash, which screen readers say inconsistently, and the metric is given once an entry, in brackets. LTS 2 is "fairly low stress (LTS 2)".
  - A stretch under 300 ft folds into its neighbour on the same street if there is one, else the longer; an LTS 3, 4 or Avoid stretch is never hidden or understated, folding only into a neighbour at least as stressful, and a folded stretch never lends its street's name or heading. That keeps 20 to 50 entries for 10 to 13 mi.
  - A control ("at a signal", "at a stop sign", "at an all-way stop", "where cross traffic stops") is said only where the junction model read the junction within 100 ft (30 m) of the turn, and "no signal mapped" only for flagged junctions; the severity words are "Higher stress junction" and "Very high stress junction".
  - Front end: `RouteDescription.tsx` (with `lib/routeDescription.ts` and its own CSS), placed with one line in `App.tsx`: a disclosure button with `aria-expanded` over an ordered list, closed unless the rider opened it before (per browser), a Copy button whose reply is the only live region, and "Download as text" built in the browser from the same sentences.
  - Timing on the live router: describing took 0.85 to 13.8 ms inside a plan before the final optimisation; a 1,200-piece benchmark now takes 0.3 ms with long stretches, 3.7 ms at worst.

**2026-10-03. The route description's stops, GPX and length (OWNER-DECISIONS 224 to 226; item 220).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 224, route description stops: '"Stop 1" (Recommended)'. Recorded with it: stops are named "Stop N" in the description, the points list and GPX.
- 225, route description in GPX: "Yes, in the route description (Recommended)". Recorded with it: the text goes in the GPX route's description.
- 226, route description length: "Yes, overview plus full (Recommended)". Recorded with it: a short overview by default merges stretches under about 0.25 mi, never hiding busy stretches or flagged junctions; a full-detail option shows the rest.

  Implemented as (ROUTE-DESCRIPTION part 2):
  - 224: "Stop N" is the label in the description ("Stop 1 at 4.7 mi (7.6 km)."), the points list, the map markers and their announcements, the "Add as stop" station button and the GPX route points. The GPX import still treats "Via N" as RouteMaker's own name, so older files re-open the same way. The API's `kind: "via"` and `via` field keep their names (the contract), described as stops.
  - 225: the route's `<rte><desc>`, the writer's existing place for the ride type, carries the ride type, a blank line, then the description as plain text, one numbered entry per line, escaped like all the file's text: the full text up to 4,000 characters, else the overview, with a label line saying which. A file is read on a device, so it does not follow the screen's view. The track's one-line `<trk><desc>` is unchanged.
  - 226: `description_overview` is a second list in the answer, beside the full `description`, so the toggle switches without a second request and a merged entry's wording (its worst tier, a facility only if all share one, the other streets) is made in one place, in Python. Stretches under 0.25 mi merge into a neighbour of their own leg, never across a stop; LTS 3 or worse merges only into a stretch at least as stressful, and a calm stretch (LTS 1 or 2) never with a busy one; a merged stretch is worded at its worst tier; a stretch that begins at a flagged junction is never merged away, and nothing is merged in front of one; stops and separate junction entries are the full list's. On five live plans the LTS 3+ distance and the flagged-junction phrases are identical in both lists.

**2026-10-03. Roundabouts (OWNER-DECISIONS 228).**

  Implemented as (FIX-BACKEND):

A roundabout is never a divided-road carriageway or a carriageway's partner, so item 109's flag no longer reads a ring two-way. The junction model takes `road_oneway` from the segment table over the router's arms (`core.junctions._with_graph`), so the fix reaches it through the rebuild.

Lanes a direction change on 55 ways (1.7 mi). Tier changes: 2 ways (0.1 mi), Marcus-David Peters Circle VA 6033428 and 132598242, LTS 3 to 4 (two lanes are a direction's: "30 mph, single lane" to "multilane"). DC and Baltimore: no tier changes; the 21 DC and 18 Baltimore roundabouts the classifier left two-way now agree with the graph. The divided flag is dropped on 4 DC ways, with no tier change; the region's divided set is 46,343 to 46,206 ways.
  - Upstream's graph makes a roundabout tagged `oneway=no` one-way too; the classifier reads the mapper's word. One such way in the extract, Kenton Court 1536402606 (residential, MD), is the only place classifier and graph differ.

**2026-10-03. Mass Ride crossing density (OWNER-DECISIONS 231, superseded the same day by 233, which 234 refines; 235 confirms the flagging rule; items 138, 171).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 231, Mass Ride crossing density: "Leave as is." Recorded with it: every LTS 3/4 crossing stays flagged on Mass Ride (items 138, 171). Superseded by 233.
- 233, amending 231: "Actually, I like merging the runs, that sounds good." Recorded with it: on Mass Ride, a run of consecutive signalised crossings along one street merges into a single entry in the junction list and the route description, e.g. "Constitution Ave: 6 signalised crossings, 2 of LTS 4 roads". Unsignalised and very-high-stress-without-signal crossings stay separate. Markers stay on the map, grouping as now.
- 234, refining 233: "I'd say we'd want some level of clumpings, especially in DC, given the diagional streets. I'd say something like a quarter mile or so of clumping." Recorded with it: Mass Ride signalised crossings are grouped by distance as well as street: consecutive signalised crossings within about 0.25 mi of the previous one join one group, even where the route changes street or crosses at a diagonal avenue or circle. The group entry names its span and streets, e.g. "1.0 to 1.6 mi: 6 signalised crossings (17th St, 15th St, ... ), 2 of LTS 4 roads". Unsignalised crossings still stand alone.

- 235, which crossings are flagged: "Unsignalled crossings only really matter if it's a higher-stress road. DC has about 10 road crossings per mile plus alleys. Not all have lights. Ideally we should be on the higher volume road anyways." Recorded with it: this confirms the rule already built (`intersections.assess`, the group reading): only crossings of, or turns involving, an LTS 3 or worse road are flagged; quiet side streets, alleys and riding along the busier road past side streets are never flagged. The 233 and 234 task adds explicit tests for it and a DC density check.

  Implemented as (POST-MERGE; routemaker/intersections.py, routemaker/describe.py, core/routing.py, core/api.py, the junction list in the front end):
  - 233 and 234, the group: `intersections.number_groups` numbers each run of two or more signalised crossings, from 1 in route order, in `Event.group`. A crossing joins when it is flagged, has a signal and is a crossing of a busy road (`GROUPED_KINDS`: `crossing` and `left_across`), and it joins the run if it is within `GROUP_WITHIN_M` (a quarter mile, 402 m) of the one before it, measured from the previous crossing and not from the first, so a chain of crossings about 1,150 ft (350 m) apart is one group however long. Since FINAL-FIX a run also ends at a stop (item 247, below). The street does not matter: diagonals and circles join, and a street crossed twice is named once. Anything else that is flagged, an unsignalised crossing, a very-high-stress crossing with no signal, a turn at a signal (`left_onto`, `left_from`, a slip lane), stands alone and ends the run. A run of one is not a group. The group reading only (`assess_route(..., group=True)`, a Mass Ride); no other ride type is touched. The cost the router weighs is unchanged, and so is every marker: every crossing is still an event and a marker, grouped on the map as before.
  - The group's severity is its worst member's (`CrossingGroup.severity`), in the existing words (Higher stress, Very high stress) and shapes (triangle, diamond; the diamond was an octagon until OWNER-DECISIONS 311). Its LTS 4 count counts LTS 4 and Avoid roads.
  - The wording (`describe.group_words`, `group_sentence`): "1.0 to 1.6 mi (1.6 to 2.6 km): 6 signalised crossings (17th Street Northwest, 15th Street Northwest, 14th Street Northwest and 3 more), 2 of LTS 4 roads". The first three streets as mapped, then "and N more"; the LTS 4 part reads "1 of them an LTS 4 road" for one, "all of LTS 4 roads" for all, and is left out for none. The description entry adds the severity in words after it ("(Very high stress junctions)."), so a colour is never the only way to hear it. A span under a tenth of a mile says its length ("2.9 mi (4.6 km), for 280 ft (84 m)"). (Since FINAL-FIX the words are "6 crossings with traffic signals" and "2 of them heavy-traffic roads (LTS 4)", "1 of them a heavy-traffic road (LTS 4)", "all of them heavy-traffic roads (LTS 4)": US spelling and the legend's words; the full description lists each crossing under the group, item 248.)
  - The description: one `junction` entry for each group in both the full list and the overview, at the first crossing, with a new `group` field (`number`, `count`, `lts4`, `streets`, `more`) and the group's severity. A crossing of a group that is also the turn into a stretch stays in that stretch's turn sentence and is counted in the group as well, so nothing is hidden. Every other entry has `group: null`.
  - The API, additive: each `intersections` row has `group` (null or the number), and `intersection_groups` lists each group with `from_m`, `to_m`, `count`, `lts4`, `streets`, `more`, `severity`, `members` (positions in `intersections`) and `text` (the phrase without the severity). The list is empty off a Mass Ride, and null where the junctions could not be read in time, as `intersections` is.
  - The junction list: a group is one row, a disclosure button (`aria-expanded`, `aria-controls`) over a list of its crossings, which are the ordinary rows and open the ordinary card. Closed to begin with; opening it announces nothing and the focus stays on the button; Enter and Space work. The row names its severity first, as every row does. Chosen over a card because the crossings are what a marshal wants to read, and a card would need a second dialog for the same words. The headline still counts every crossing. Tested in a browser (scripts/a11y/check.mjs, section 6): the keyboard, the accessible names and states, the Tab order closed and open, the card from a member, and 320 px.
  - 235, the tests (tests/test_crossing_groups.py, `TestFlaggingOnAMassRide`): no crossing of an LTS 1 or 2 road, an unknown-tier road or an alley is flagged, signalised or not, in any movement; riding along the busier road past side streets, or past a lesser busy road with the right of way, is never flagged; a mile of ten side streets and alleys along an LTS 4 road gives no events.
  - Density, measured on the post-merge branch through the read-only forwarder on the live standard router, Mass Ride, weekend: Lincoln Memorial to the Capitol, flags per mile and list entries before and after grouping. Constitution Ave, direct (2.52 mi): 9 flags (3.6 a mile), 9 entries, 3 after (1.2 a mile). The same with three vias on Constitution Ave (3.20 mi): 13 flags (4.1 a mile), 13 entries, 7 after. Pennsylvania Ave NW from Washington Circle with three vias (3.52 mi): 23 flags (6.5 a mile), 23 entries, 5 after. Pennsylvania Ave direct (2.78 mi): 23 flags (8.3 a mile), 23 entries, 3 after. Every flag on the two Pennsylvania routes is signalised, and the biggest group is 15 crossings in 1.4 mi.
  - Open for the owner: a group is not split at a stop (a via point), so a group can run across "Stop N". The stops are the description's own entries, in order by where each begins. (Superseded: the owner chose "Split at stops", item 247, built in FINAL-FIX; see the entry for 246 to 250.)

**2026-10-03. The final-fix round (OWNER-DECISIONS 246, 247, 248, 249, 250; the six-reviewer re-check of 2b0cf00).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 246, DC two-way records against OSM one-way (row C4) on Mass Ride: "Mass Ride keeps one-way (Recommended)". Recorded with it: Solo graphs (standard, weekend, e-bike) follow DC's two-way. On the no-trail graph a street stays one-way if either source says so.

  Implemented as (FINAL-FIX): `pipeline.variants.routing_for` hands each variant the District's routing keys; on the no-trail graph a two-way record (`oneway=no`) is not laid over a way OSM has one-way (`is_motor_oneway`, a roundabout included), so the way keeps OSM's one-way and the closure (`close_contraflow`, items 192, 219) then closes its reverse; every other key of the record still applies, and the solo graphs take all of it (item 190). Row B and the filled one-ways are one-way on every graph already, so a street is one-way on Mass Ride's graph if either source says so. The classifier and the segment table's `road_*` columns are shared by every variant and follow the solo graphs: on a Mass Ride such a street is read two-way at a junction (its lanes doubled, oncoming traffic on a left, a left-across marker), which only ever overstates a junction. Tests: tests/test_variants.py (`routing_for`), tests/test_lua_remap.py (row C4 read back through the transform, with and against the line), tests/test_pipeline_end_to_end.py (`test_a_district_two_way_record_is_every_graph_s_direction`, and the helper `_assert_graph_and_traits_agree`, which now holds the no-trail graph to "one-way if either source says so").

  Also in this round (correctness re-check, blocker 1): the segment table's `road_oneway` is the graph's own direction (`StressResult.graph_oneway`, `tags.is_oneway` of the tags the classifier read, a roundabout included), not item 109's relief reading (`StressResult.oneway`, False on a divided road's carriageway), which had made every carriageway two-way for the junction model once the rebuild wrote the column. Probed on the real tags of Connecticut Ave NW 6051314 (3 lanes one way, 25 mph, LTS 3) and five more carriageways of the divided set (Ohio Drive SW, Suitland Parkway SE, Park Heights Avenue, Perring Parkway, North Point Boulevard): the pair crossing now has the median-refuge credit (Connecticut Ave 1,440 ft to 1,080 ft unsignalized, 150 to 112 ft at a signal), its marker says "3-lane" (not "6-lane"), and a Mass Ride's left off it draws no left-across marker.

- 247, Mass Ride crossing groups and stops: "Split at stops (Recommended)".

  Implemented as (FINAL-FIX): `intersections.number_groups(events, stops)` closes a run at each stop (a crossing at the stop begins the next leg's run), `assess_route` passes them, and `core.routing.plan` numbers the route's events again with the leg ends along the traced length (the events' own measure), so no group spans "Stop N" in the junction list, `intersection_groups` or either description. Live, Mass Ride Lincoln Memorial to the Capitol with three stops on Constitution Ave: the group that ran 1.4 to 1.7 mi across Stop 2 (1.6 mi) is now 2 crossings before it, and the third crossing stands alone after it. Pennsylvania Ave NW with three stops: the 15-crossing group across Stop 3 is now 6 and 9. Tests: tests/test_crossing_groups.py `TestSplitAtStops` (the re-check's case: crossings at 500 to 1,100 m, about 0.3 to 0.7 mi, and a stop at 800 m, 0.5 mi, give two groups either side of "Stop 1"), tests/test_route_description.py `test_a_mass_ride_group_is_split_at_the_stop`.

- 248, group detail: "Yes, in full detail (Recommended)". Recorded with it: In full detail and the text/GPX download, a group lists its crossings with their mile markers as sub-entries. The overview keeps one line per group.

  Implemented as (FINAL-FIX): the full description's group entry carries `group.crossings`, each `{from_m, from_mi, street, severity, crossed_tier, text}`, the text worded as a junction entry ("At 1.4 mi (2.2 km): Cross 15th Street Northwest (LTS 3) at a signal (Higher stress junction)."); the overview's entry has `crossings: null` (`describe.group_crossings`, `core.api.DescriptionCrossingOut`). The page lists them as a nested ordered list under the group, named "Crossings in this group, in route order"; Copy, Download and the GPX `<desc>` set them in under the group ("   3.1. At 1.4 mi ..."). Every street of a group is so named somewhere a screen reader reaches; the overview and the junction list keep "and N more".

- 249, the spec re-check's standing recommendations: "Accept all (Recommended)". Recorded with it: signalised turns end a group; the GPX overview switch at 4,000 characters; "to" instead of a dash, and "fairly low stress (LTS 2)"; markers say "no signal mapped"; MoCo 409/411 and Baltimore 256/264 override rows as loaded; the trail-credit fade with the slider; the current cost constants (CALM_RATE_MAX 10, detour tiers, intersection feet values, LTS 3 left at 600 ft) for the beta, re-measured after rider suggestions (206).

  Implemented as: nothing to change; each is as built. Noted with them (the spec re-check's open note, kept as built): an LTS 3 stretch merged into an LTS 4 overview entry is worded at LTS 4 (`describe._group_run` words a merged entry at its most stressful tier). It overstates, never understates; the full description has each stretch at its own tier.

- 250, Trailmaxxing and LTS 4: "For trail maxxing, weight LTS4 roads as a much higher penalty. It's putting me on dangerous roads just for a little more trails." Recorded with it: Observed on the live site (code 0ec8fdc, graph 20260927). That build has no calm search or trail credit; its Trailmaxxing is use_roads 0 with graded LTS 4. For Trailmaxxing (and Cargo with passengers, per the 241 rationale): the calm-search exposure weights rise to LTS 3 x1, LTS 4 x8, Avoid x16 (from 1/2/3); the trail credit can never buy LTS 4 or Avoid metres: a candidate with more LTS 4+ metres than the router's own route is refused outright, on top of the 2% + 164 ft guard on weighted exposure.

  Implemented as (FINAL-FIX): `presets.Exposure` is a preset field (`Preset.exposure`, and `Preset.carrying_exposure` for Cargo Bike's "Cargo with passengers"): `EXPOSURE_STRESS_AVERSE` (1, 8, 16, with the LTS 4 hold) on Trailmaxxing and Cargo with passengers, `EXPOSURE_STANDARD` (1, 2, 3, no hold) on every other ride and on carrying cargo; `presets.exposure_for` picks it and `core.routing.plan` hands it to the search (`refine.Context.exposure`). The weights are the search's exposure everywhere it is read: the score, the Traffic-wins guard and the seek's corridor scoring. The hold (`refine.more_lts4`) refuses a candidate with more LTS 4 and Avoid metres than the router's first route (1 m of slack, a trace's rounding), for the whole trip and for each leg, in the exclusion rounds, the wide search, each leg of the seek and the spliced trip; `calm_search` reports `lts4_before_m` and `lts4_after_m`, and a refused seek candidate's outcome is `more_lts4`.

  Measured on the twelve trail-seek trips, Trailmaxxing at 100, through the read-only forwarder on the live routers (graph 20260927): LTS 4 miles, trail miles, total miles and time, for the router's own route (what live answers: it has no search), the 2b0cf00 search and FINAL-FIX. In total: the router's own 26.7 mi of LTS 4, 44.0 mi of trail, 116.0 mi, 618 min; 2b0cf00 20.6 mi, 60.5 mi, 130.4 mi, 694 min; FINAL-FIX 20.8 mi, 56.4 mi, 127.2 mi, 675 min. The router's own route does use LTS 4 where a calmer way of modest extra length exists (Takoma to Hyattsville 1.49 mi of LTS 4 to none for 1.3 mi more; Eastern Market to Anacostia 1.33 to 0.25 for 0.9 mi; Silver Spring to College Park 1.09 to 0.26 for 1.7 mi; Rockville to Silver Spring 0.76 to 0.21 for 2.6 mi; Laurel to College Park 3.07 to 1.26 for 3.1 mi), so live's behaviour, the router's route alone, was the cause, and the search finds these. On no trip did either search end with more LTS 4 than the router's route; the hold refused both seek candidates on Bethesda to Silver Spring, which would have added LTS 4 for trail. Poolesville to Darnestown (4.87 mi) and Bowie to Annapolis (11.93 mi) keep their LTS 4: no calmer route was found. Details in docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "The final-fix round".

**2026-10-03. FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM (OWNER-DECISIONS 256 to 266).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 256, Trailmaxxing for long trips, owner 2026-10-03: "Don't just add that to the backlog, that should be a top part of the next release. Trailmaxxing should reduce the stress as much as possible and allow long detours. Actually being on trails is less of an issue. Something like programming in the max distance ir something." FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM, a top item for the next release:
  - Trailmaxxing's goal is the lowest stress possible, with long detours allowed.
  - Remove the calm search's 30 km (19 mi) span limit for Trailmaxxing; plan long trips leg by leg internally, with a time budget sized to the trip.
  - Add a rider-set maximum distance, e.g. "up to 60 mi". The planner minimises stress (LTS 4/Avoid first, then LTS 3, then crossings) within that distance, instead of a fixed detour rate.
  - Trail miles matter less than stress, so the trail credit (202) becomes secondary or is dropped, pending the owner's answer.
  Prompted by the Union Station to Baltimore Penn Station plan (58.0 mi, 0.24 mi LTS 4), which needed manual leg splitting.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Long calm trips, the target distance and the routes to choose from"; the dial's name, its meaning and the default's limit are amended by 267 to 271, below): a Trailmaxxing plan at the top of the slider past 19 mi (30 km) of straight line, and under the long-ride confirm span (93 mi, 150 km), is planned leg by leg (`core.refine.refine_long`, `core.legsplit`). The router's own route for the whole trip is asked for and traced; it is cut into legs of about 7.5 mi (12 km) of straight line at points on that route, on calm stretches where there are some; each leg's own route is asked for; the legs with LTS 3, 4 or Avoid are searched worst first, each with its share of the time by its weight; the legs go back together as one trip (the plan's own legs in the answer: `leg_ends`, the description and `stops` are the plan's) and the whole route's junctions are read once. If the whole is not as calm as the router's own route, or nothing changed, the router's own route is answered. The time is the long ride's 50 s in all (47 s for the router and traces; gunicorn's timeout is 60 s), kept rather than a new figure; an ordinary plan keeps 40 s; a long calm plan takes the long ride's in-flight slot as well (one at a time in the deployment). Other ride types keep the 19 mi limit. The "Target distance" (named "Longest ride" until item 271) is an optional dial at the top of the slider (`target_distance_m` in the API, `targetmi` in the link, miles first with kilometres in brackets, a number field in the Adjust panel, 0.7 to 621 mi), ignored below the top. Unset, the ceiling is 1.6 times the router's own route and at least a mile more (`presets.default_ceiling_m`). The search never takes a route past the ceiling (not even reading one), and a long plan shares its detour among its legs where it buys most (`refine.choose_options`). Where the router's own route is past the target, the router is asked at lower traffic positions (70, 40, 0) and between the last two (3 bisections) until a route fits, and the calmest that fits is the first route (`calm_search.fitted_at`), unless a calmer route past the target is worth its miles (271); if none fits, the least stressful found is answered with `calm_search.fits` false and a note (267). The link version stays 2 (additive fields). Measured: Union Station to Baltimore Penn at 60 mi 58.6 mi, 0.26 mi of LTS 4, 6.94 mi of LTS 3, 1 red and 30 orange junctions, planned in 11.8 s with 138 router calls; at its default 73.2 mi, 0.33 mi of LTS 4, 6.01 mi of LTS 3, 0 red and 37 orange junctions, planned in 16.8 s with 184 router calls; at 50 mi 47.7 mi, 2.94 mi of LTS 4, 12.19 mi of LTS 3, 3 red and 16 orange junctions, planned in 19.3 s with 186 router calls; at 47 mi 46.9 mi, 4.70 mi of LTS 4, 24.47 mi of LTS 3, 1 red and 14 orange junctions, planned in 29.2 s with 343 router calls; against the router alone (live: 57.7 mi, 0.26 mi of LTS 4, 7.24 mi of LTS 3). Bethesda to Frederick 53.7 mi, 1.81 mi of LTS 4, 5.31 mi of LTS 3, 4 red and 27 orange junctions, planned in 9.7 s with 97 router calls; Alexandria to Annapolis 72.0 mi, 1.49 mi of LTS 4, 8.48 mi of LTS 3, 2 red and 33 orange junctions, planned in 20.3 s with 187 router calls. The twelve trail-seek trips: in total the old code gave 169.0 mi, 4.21 mi of LTS 4, 16.15 mi of LTS 3, 11 red and 73 orange junctions; the new code 167.7 mi, 4.21 mi of LTS 4, 16.20 mi of LTS 3, 11 red and 73 orange junctions (docs/DEVELOPMENT.md has the table). The seek's corridor search was kept (it was taken on 3 of 6 of the measured long plans).

- 257, Trail bonus, owner 2026-10-03: "Drop it (Recommended)". This supersedes 202. Trailmaxxing minimises stress within the rider's distance limit, and trails win only because they are low stress. A quiet street counts the same as a trail. Part of FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM (256), next release. It is planner-only and needs no rebuild.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): `Preset.trail_credit`, `presets.TRAIL_CREDIT`, `presets.trail_credit_for`, `refine.trail_flags`, the score's trail term and the corridor search's trail term (`MIN_TRAIL_GAIN_M`, the credit in `best_in`) are removed; `calm_search` no longer reports `trail_credit`, `trail_before_m` or `trail_after_m`. A corridor is worth the busy road it replaces less its detour; the exclusion rounds and the corridors both reach trails only by being low stress. The Trailmaxxing card reads "The calmest route, aiming at your target distance ... It finds the least stressful route, not the most trail, so it may add miles" (since 271), and the calm note at the top says "A quiet street counts the same as a trail". 202 is superseded; 203 (the seek leg by leg) stands. Not a rebuild.

- 258, Stress priority order for FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM, owner 2026-10-03: "The priority should actually be LTS4+>Very Stressful Crossings>LTS3>Stressful Crossings". This amends 256's order. Minimise, in strict priority:
  1. LTS 4 and Avoid metres;
  2. very high stress (red) junctions;
  3. LTS 3 metres;
  4. higher stress (orange) junctions;
  then distance, within the rider's maximum.

  Implemented as: superseded by 259 to 262 in the order built (below); the order is `core.refine.better`, a strict comparison with a tolerance at each level.

- 259, Owner 2026-10-03, amending 258: "Or weight Very Stressful and LTS4 equally". Asked for the common unit, the owner chose "Its own junction cost (Recommended)". The top priority is one figure: LTS 4 and Avoid feet plus the junction model's cost (ft-equivalent, about 2,000-4,500) of each very high stress (red) junction. Then LTS 3 feet, then orange junctions, then distance, within the rider's maximum.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): the top figure is LTS 4 and Avoid metres plus the junction model's cost of each very high stress (red) junction, converted from feet-equivalent (`Analysis.top_m`, `red_m`), compared with a 15 m (about 50 ft) tolerance, so a trivial gain at the top does not force a large loss below it. **This amends the item 250 hold** (as the coordinator recorded): a candidate is refused only if its top figure (LTS 4 and Avoid plus red junction cost) is more than the router's own route's by 1 m, whole trip and each leg (`refine.top_by_leg`, `more_lts4`), so 1,000 ft of LTS 4 may be traded for avoiding a 3,000 ft red junction, and more LTS 4 for no junction gain is still refused (tests in tests/test_longcalm.py). The hills slider's choice of an alternate (`routing.calmer_or_own`, `no_busier_than_middle`) holds the LTS 4 and Avoid metres alone: it has no junction events.

- 260, Owner 2026-10-03: "Make LTS3 and orange crossing the same." By analogy with 259, the second priority is one figure: LTS 3 feet plus each higher stress (orange) junction's cost (ft-equivalent, about 600-2,000). Final order: (1) LTS 4 + Avoid feet + red junction costs; (2) LTS 3 feet + orange junction costs; (3) distance, within the rider's maximum.

  Implemented as: the second figure is LTS 3 metres plus the cost of each higher stress (orange) junction (`Analysis.second_m`, `orange_m`), tolerance 50 m (about a block): a 1,000 ft-cost orange junction counts as 1,000 ft of LTS 3 (tests).

- 261, Owner 2026-10-03: "Elevation preferences before distance." Final FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM order, within the rider's maximum distance:
  1. LTS 4 + Avoid feet + red junction costs;
  2. LTS 3 feet + orange junction costs;
  3. the rider's hills preference from the hills slider: left of the detent, less climbing, weighted by the existing climb cost (steeper counts more); at the detent, ignored; right of the detent, more climbing, until FOLLOWUP-HILLS-TOLERATE (242) changes its meaning;
  4. distance.

  Implemented as: built, then superseded by 262 before it shipped: its lexicographic hills level is replaced by the effort-equivalent distance as the third level.

- 262, Owner 2026-10-03, replacing 261's lexicographic hills level: "Instead how about something like hill equivalent distance? For instance if a road would typically require 50 kJ at a given distance, but a typical person would need 100 kJ given the elevation, that counts twice as much. The slider just affects how much that factors in." So the FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM tiebreaker below the stress levels becomes effort-equivalent distance, not actual distance:
  - estimate the energy (kJ) a typical rider spends on each segment from its length, grade and a typical speed/weight (a standard cycling-power model: rolling resistance + air + climbing m*g*grade);
  - the effort-equivalent distance of a segment is its length scaled by (energy with elevation / energy on the flat for that length);
  - the hills slider blends between actual distance (detent or "don't care") and full effort-equivalent distance (max avoid). Right of the detent (prefer climbing) inverts it, pending FOLLOWUP-HILLS-TOLERATE (242).
  Final order: (1) LTS 4+Avoid ft + red junction cost; (2) LTS 3 ft + orange junction cost; (3) effort-equivalent distance per the slider. The rider's maximum "Longest ride" (256) stays in ACTUAL miles, since that is what they will physically ride.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): the third level is `(1 - w) x actual + w x effort-equivalent distance` (`refine.level3`), w from the Hills slider (0 at the detent, 1 at full avoid, `Context.hills_weight`), tolerance 50 m. The effort of a stretch is its length times the force on its grade over the force on the flat at a steady 12.4 mph (20 km/h) in the standard cycling-power model (rolling resistance + air + climbing m g sin t; `routemaker.effort`, citing Martin et al. 1998), over 300 m windows of the router's elevation profile, floored at the flat so a descent costs the flat and never offsets a climb. 8% costs 6.5 times the flat. Right of the detent (Hills set to seek hills), since item 298(3), the stress order (258 to 262) and the target (267 to 271) still apply and only the effort tiebreak inverts: the third level weighs effort against the climbing the rider seeks (`refine.level3` with `Context.hills_seek_weight`), so of two routes level on stress the hillier is preferred; at the top of the stress slider no climb search among the router's alternatives is asked and the answer's `hills_seek.limited` is `"calm_first"` (298(3), below). What seeking should mean in the end is still FOLLOWUP-HILLS-TOLERATE (242). The "Longest ride" stays in actual metres. The brake-grade and hills-avoid guards are unchanged.

- 263, Effort model parameters, owner 2026-10-03: rider plus bike about 90 kg (200 lb), "Typical commuter + bike ~90 kg (Recommended)"; descents "Floor at coasting (Recommended)" (a segment's effort never drops below the flat/coasting energy for its length, so descents are cheap but never offset climbs). The energy model and these constants are a documented assumption, tunable later and a candidate per-ride input.

  Implemented as: the constants are named in `routemaker.effort` (90 kg, Crr 0.006, CdA 0.40 m^2, air 1.225 kg/m^3, 20 km/h) with their sources; descents are floored at the flat (tests: a descent costs 1.0, never offsets a climb).

- 264, Owner 2026-10-03, on the effort model (262/263): "And the highest would be a heavy person on a fully loaded touring bike. The lightest would be someone on a UCI min bike." So the ~90 kg reference (263) becomes an optional rider input, a total system weight (rider + bike + cargo):
  - lightest about 68 kg: a light rider (~61 kg) on a UCI-minimum 6.8 kg bike;
  - heaviest about 140 kg: a heavy rider on a fully loaded touring bike (~20-25 kg loaded);
  - default ~90 kg (263) when unset; Cargo with passengers defaults heavier.
  Weight genuinely changes the result: climbing power scales with mass but aero (CdA) does not, so a heavier/loaded rider's hills cost relatively more effort-distance. Input is in lb first (kg in brackets); it is a candidate for a saved rider profile later.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): an optional input, `system_weight_kg` in the API (68 to 140 as first built; 25 to 450 since 337, and 25 to 700, clamped silently, since 352), in the link until 313 took it out, "System weight (pounds)" in the Adjust panel at the top of the slider (150 to 309 lb as first built, kilograms in brackets), now the private rider and bike weight of 313-318; default 90 kg (198 lb), 120 kg (265 lb) for Cargo with passengers. Only the climbing and rolling terms scale with it, so a flat route's effort is its length at any weight and the same hilly-against-flat tie resolves more strongly toward flat at 140 kg than at 68 kg (tests/test_effort.py).

- 265, Owner 2026-10-03: "Also maybe allow several routes to be shown in this case. The rider would probably pick the most scenic." For FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM, at the calm top end, return up to 3-4 candidate routes instead of one.
  - Each is within the stress order (258-262) and the rider's maximum distance.
  - "Present options, don't score scenery (Recommended)": do not rank scenery; show each route's stats (LTS mix, trail miles, climbing/effort, distance, red/orange counts) and let the rider judge scenery from the map. No scenery dataset.
  - "Meaningfully different corridors (Recommended)": only offer a route that differs substantially from those already shown (a different trail or river valley, not a minor reroute); measure overlap and drop near-duplicates.
  - Each candidate is still held to the item 259/260 combined figures and the 250 hold.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): at the top of the slider the answer carries up to three more routes (`candidates`, each a whole route body with its `rank`; the answer is rank 1 and unchanged, so old clients are unaffected). Each is within the ceiling (and no further past the rider's target than the answer, since 271) and the hold, a near-tie with the answer on stress (top figure within 45 m, second within 300 m), and meaningfully different from every route already offered: **under 70% shared road (60% until item 269) by matched way length, or at least 5 mi (8 km) of different road** (`refine.pick_candidates`; the second test is the owner's "different corridor", since 70% of a 58 mi route is never different). Scenery is not scored. Each carries its stats (LTS mix, trail miles, red and orange junctions, climb, `effort_m`, distance). They are read in full inside the budget (6 s left for each); fewer are returned when time is short, and a trip with one obvious corridor returns one route. A plan of more than one leg (a loop, or a ride with stops) never offers one leg's seek candidate as a route of its own: every route offered is the whole trip, with the answer's legs, start and end (release review, correctness B1). The page shows a radio group, "Routes to choose from", only where there is more than one; choosing draws that route and its description and announces "Route 2 of 3." once settled (the a11y check). Measured: 1 of the 12 trail-seek trips returned more than one route (bethesda-capitol); the long trips returned one.

- 266, Owner 2026-10-03: "Another one that I often like on my fun rides is to turn an out and back into a loop." Folded into FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM.
  - Trigger "Both: detect + a toggle": when start and end are the same point, default to returning by a different way; also offer a "Make it a loop" control on an out-and-back to a destination (plan start->dest, then dest->start avoiding the outbound).
  - Overlap "Prefer, not force": strongly favour a different return, but allow shared stretches where there is no reasonable alternative (a single bridge, a one-access trail), by penalising rather than excluding the outbound edges. Report the overlap percentage.
  - The loop still obeys the stress order (258-262), the effort tiebreaker and the rider's maximum ACTUAL distance (the whole loop, not each leg).
  - Combine with 265: the alternates can be distinct loops.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): a ride whose last point is its first (within 50 m) is a loop, and `loop: true` (a "Make it a loop" checkbox in the Adjust panel, off by default on a point-to-point ride, on and fixed on a round trip, `loop=1` in the link) closes a point-to-point ride on its start; not on a Mass Ride (the owner kept it so, 298(4), below). The way back is asked for alone with points along the way out excluded (every 120 m, none within 500 m of an end or stop, thinned to every 2nd, 4th and 8th point if the router has no route or the whole loop would be past the ceiling), so the overlap is preferred away, not forced; every thinning is asked (up to four routes), and the least stressful of the ways back sharing at most 30% of their road with the way out (matched by way) is taken, otherwise the one sharing least; where the way back is the way out whatever is excluded (90% or more) the router's own route stays and `loop.fallback` says `out_and_back`. `loop` reports `overlap_pct`, `shared_m`, `return_m`, the points excluded and the routes asked. The search after it keeps the way back different, and the target distance, its ceiling and the diminishing returns are the whole loop's (271). The alternates of 265 can be distinct loops. A loop over 19 mi (30 km) of straight line in all is not searched (`limited: "span"`): a loop is never a long calm plan, and its straight line counts the way back too, so a loop with a 9.5 mi out-leg gets no calm search and its note says the trip is over 19 mi in a straight line. Measured: loop-takoma-hyattsville: 8.8 mi, 17.4% of the way back shared, fallback None, 2.8 s; loop-bethesda-silver-spring: 11.4 mi, 21.6% of the way back shared, fallback None, 7.8 s; round-trip-tysons-ballston: 21.3 mi, 7.9% of the way back shared, fallback None, 8.9 s; loop-rockville-silver-spring-60mi-cap-12: 21.1 mi, 88.1% of the way back shared, fallback out_and_back, 3.9 s; loop-default-preset-bethesda-capitol: 25.4 mi, 1.5% of the way back shared, fallback None, 4.7 s.

- 267, Owner 2026-10-03, LONG-CALM no-fit: "Least-stress route, flagged (Recommended)". When no route fits the rider's distance, return the calmest route found (still in the 258-262 order), flagged "over your target by X mi", not the shortest.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): where no route the router gives fits the target (the router's own, the ladder's rungs and the bisections), the least stressful of them within the 1.25x ceiling (271) is answered, by `refine.calmer` and the shorter on a tie (`routing._past_target`), with `calm_search.fits` false, `limited: "target_distance"` and `over_target_m`; the page says "No route within your target distance (X) was found. This is the least stressful one found. It is Y mi over your target." Where none is within the ceiling the least stressful of all the routes found is answered, flagged the same way (item 298(2), "Calmest found, flagged (Recommended)", which replaced the shortest the first build answered there; the shortest only where none can be read). `calm_search.no_fit` is true wherever no route within the target was found (false where one was, null with no target), and the page's "No route within your target distance" sentence is keyed on it; `limited: "ceiling"` is a different thing, the exclusion search stopping because its next round found only routes past the ceiling. Measured: the owner's ride at 47 mi answers 56.6 mi with 0.26 mi of LTS 4, 9.6 mi over.

- 268, Owner 2026-10-03, LONG-CALM default cap: "Diminishing returns (Recommended)". 1.6x the baseline stays the ceiling, but extra distance is taken only when it buys a meaningful stress cut. Roughly: at least 1 mi of LTS 3 saved per 5 mi added, LTS 4/Avoid and red junctions weighted higher. Constants are documented and tunable.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Diminishing returns on extra distance"): at the top of the slider a longer route must be calmer and save, in metres of LTS 3 (`refine.stress_weight_m`: LTS 3 x1, LTS 4 x2, Avoid x3, a red junction's cost x2, an orange junction's x1, `refine.WORTH_WEIGHTS`), at least the metres it adds over 5 (`refine.WORTH_DEFAULT`); a shorter route replaces a calmer longer one that was not worth its miles. In the exclusion rounds, the seek (leg by leg at the whole trip's length), a long plan's sharing of the detour (`choose_options`, at the whole trip's length) and a loop's way back. The level weights are the standard 1/2/3, not the stress-averse 1/8/16, with which one red junction bought 23 mi and Union Station to Penn stayed 73.2 mi; the owner confirmed the standard weights in 287(1), below. Since the release review (spec SF2) the distance is charged against the highest level that improves: an LTS 4 (top figure) saving pays for its miles on its own and extra LTS 3 never subtracts from it; within a level the 1/2/3 weights still set how many miles a saving buys. Measured: Union Station to Penn at its default 58.6 mi (from 73.2 mi), 0.26 mi of LTS 4, 6.94 mi of LTS 3; Alexandria to Annapolis 65.2 mi (from 72.0).

- 269, Owner 2026-10-03, LONG-CALM alternates: "Loosen a little (Recommended)". The "meaningfully different" overlap threshold goes from 60% to about 70% shared, and near-duplicates are still dropped.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): `refine.ALT_OVERLAP` is 0.70; the 5 mi (8 km) different-road test and the near-tie bands are unchanged. Measured: still 1 of the 12 trail-seek trips offers a second route (bethesda-capitol): the others' readable alternatives are not near-ties on stress, so the overlap is not what limits them.

- 270, Owner 2026-10-03, old-graph throwaway router: "Skip it (Recommended)". Not run; the new planner already avoids the FIT ride's LTS 4 corridor.

- 271, Owner 2026-10-03: "Change the name from max distance to target distance, because it can get longer". The rider's "Longest ride" / max distance (256) becomes a TARGET distance: a soft goal, not a hard cap. The planner aims at or under it, but may exceed it when that buys a meaningful stress cut (the 268 diminishing-returns rule applies past the target), and always flags the overage (267). Rename everywhere: UI label "Target distance", API max_distance_m -> target_distance_m, link param maxmi -> targetmi, limited "max_distance" -> "target_distance". The links are unreleased, so there is no back-compat alias. Miles first (feet/km in brackets).

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): renamed in the API (`target_distance_m`; `calm_search.target_distance_m`, `target_distance_set`, `limited: "target_distance"`; the old name is refused as an unknown field), the link (`targetmi`, version still 2), the Adjust panel ("Target distance (miles)"), the code, tests and docs. Up to the target distance is free; past it a longer route must save stress at the stricter 1 mi of LTS 3 per 2.5 mi past the target (`refine.WORTH_OVER_TARGET`), and never past 1.25 times the target (`presets.TARGET_CEILING_RATIO`, a hard ceiling); where the router's own route is past the target it is weighed against the calmest route that fits by the same rule (`routing._past_target`). The answer always gives `calm_search.over_target_m` (0 within it, null with no target), `ceiling_m`, and each candidate's `over_target_m`; the page says "X mi (Y km) over your target" in the summary, in the announcement and on each route to choose from, and no candidate goes further past the target than the answer. A loop's target is the whole loop's. Measured: Union Station to Penn at 60 mi 58.6 mi (within it); the owner's ride at 60 mi 57.2 mi with 0.26 mi of LTS 4; Union Station to Penn at 50 and 47 mi answers its 57.5 mi calm route, 7.5 and 10.5 mi over, with 0.26 mi of LTS 4 (the routes that fit had 2.94 and 4.70).

- 272, Owner 2026-10-03: "One issue that I noted was that one router from the map file kept dodging back and forth into side streets along a busier road. That would only make sense if it bought a meaningful distance of calm." FOLLOWUP-DEDODGE: when a route leaves a road for side streets and rejoins the same corridor, keep that dodge only if it avoids a meaningful length of higher-stress riding. Default is at least ~0.25 mi of the higher tier avoided per dodge (a quarter mile, as with crossing clumping), tunable. Weigh it against the dodge's extra turns and junctions (ties into FOLLOWUP-TURN-LOAD 254). Otherwise stay on the main road. Applies to all presets, planner-side, no graph rebuild. Report how many dodges are removed on the standard trips and on Union Station->Penn.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-DEDODGE; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "No dodging through side streets (FOLLOWUP-DEDODGE, items 272, 273)"): `core.dedodge`, run on every ride type after the search and before the answer, on the answer's route alone (not on a loop's way back, 266, and not on the routes to choose from, which are picked again against the changed answer: `dedodge.settle`). A dodge is a stretch of streets (nothing else: a trail, a crossing or steps is not one) that leaves a named road and comes back to the same corridor within 1 mi [1.6 km]: the same road by name, or a road that runs on from it in its direction within 150 m [490 ft] of its line. One under 50 m [160 ft], one of unnamed edges alone under 60 m [200 ft], or one with fewer than two turns is no weave and is not checked (`skipped`). The route between the dodge's ends without the dodge's streets is asked of the router (the plan's own request and graph, each of the dodge's edges excluded) and read whole, junctions included, beside the route as it was. On every ride type, Default included, the dodge is kept if it avoids more than 160 ft [50 m] of the higher stress, the second level's tie step (`dedodge.TIE_STEP_M`, `refine.MAXCALM_STEPS[1]`; the stress is the extra LTS 4, Avoid and red-junction cost plus the extra LTS 3 and orange-junction cost the main road would carry, the two figures of items 258 to 260), with no turn charge (item 298(1), "Same as Trailmaxxing (Recommended)", `dedodge.TIE_RULE_ALL_PRESETS`). The first build used that step at the top of the slider only and, below it, a quarter of a mile [400 m] (`MIN_AVOIDED_M`) plus 260 ft [80 m] for every turn past two (`TURN_CHARGE_M`); 298(1) amends 272's 0.25 mi, so Default no longer gives back up to 0.34 mi of LTS 3 a dodge for a shorter route. A dodge that avoids nothing, as on Konterra Drive, is still taken out everywhere. Otherwise the main road's stretch is spliced in. Whatever that says, a dodge is never taken out where the route would carry more LTS 4, Avoid or red-junction cost (more than the hold's metre of slack over the leg as the pass found it: the order of 258 to 262 and the hold of 250 are never broken), be longer (the target and ceiling of 267 to 271 only get easier), or be worse on the Hills slider's blended distance. Bounds, per plan (one pass a plan): at most 8 checks, 40 exclusions to a request, 5 s, ending 6 s before the plan's deadline, 8 s to a call; each check is 1 or 2 `/route` calls (where the router finds no path facing the way the route goes it is asked again without headings), a `trace_attributes` of the whole spliced leg and its junctions' `/locate` (50 nodes a call, twice). A reading the pass's clock cut short is not used. The answer says what was found and done in `dodges`, and `calm_search` gives the route as answered. Measured (r1): Trailmaxxing takes 2 dodges out of the twelve trips, each avoiding nothing (Konterra Drive; Lottsford Vista Road), LTS 3 16.20 mi with the pass off and 16.24 with it; Default, on the first build's quarter mile, took 6 out, avoiding 0 to 0.34 mi each (Dicus Mill Road's 0.34 mi removed by its turn charge), LTS 3 16.80 to 18.04 mi for 1.2 mi less riding, which is why 298(1) put Default on the tie step; Union Station to Penn 58.70 to 58.61 mi (one Van Dusen Road dodge, avoiding nothing), LTS 4 unchanged everywhere.

- 273, Owner 2026-10-03, test case for 272: "It was along Konterra Drive" (Laurel/Beltsville, PG County, beside I-95 near Contee Rd). The saved Union->Penn plan (bmore-split4.json) uses Konterra for only 0.14 mi (mile 6.45-6.59 of leg 2), and the owner's FIT ride doesn't use it at all. So the developer must reproduce the weaving with live /api/route runs along the Konterra Dr corridor (end to end in both directions, plus the Union->Penn plan on the current planner, with and without a 60 mi target) before fixing. Use it as the regression case.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-DEDODGE): reproduced on the live routers. North on Konterra Drive (-76.88905, 39.06785 to -76.89068, 39.08179) the Default, Trailmaxxing and Cargo plans leave Konterra Drive at -76.885651, 39.074958 (way 256386638) for Virginia Manor Road (ways 235061913, 1473496057, 240334415) and rejoin Konterra Drive on the same way 0.25 mi [408 m] later: both are LTS 3, so the dodge avoids nothing, adds 3 turns and 0.09 mi [147 m], and the Fast, Group Ride, Mass Ride and E-bike plans stay on Konterra Drive. 1.84 mi [2.97 km] becomes 1.75 mi [2.82 km], the Fast plan's route. The southbound plans do not dodge. The saved Union Station to Penn plan's 0.14 mi on Konterra Drive is not a dodge. The recorded answers are tests/data/dedodge_konterra.json and `tests/test_dedodge.py`, `TestKonterraDrive`.

**2026-10-03. The LONG-CALM r1 questions (OWNER-DECISIONS 287).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented. Parts (1) to (3) confirm what the first build did and was asking about (items 267, 268 and 271, above).

- 287(1), Owner 2026-10-03, LONG-CALM r1 questions: "Normal weights (Recommended)": the worth-it stress weights for 268 stay at the standard 1/2/3, not Trailmaxxing's 1/8/16. LTS 4 still ranks first in the stress order; these weights only govern how many extra miles a stress saving buys.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM): `refine.WORTH_WEIGHTS` is LTS 3 x1, LTS 4 x2, Avoid x3 (with each red junction's cost at the LTS 4 weight and each orange one's at the LTS 3 weight) on every ride, Trailmaxxing included; the stress-averse 1/8/16 stay the search's exposure (item 250), not its price on distance. Since the release review (spec SF2; `refine.stress_saved_m`) the weights are read as the record of 287(1) says: the distance is charged against the highest level that improves, so an LTS 4 saving pays for its miles on its own and extra LTS 3 never subtracts from it, and within a level the 1/2/3 weights set the trade; a shorter route with LTS 4 no longer replaces an LTS 4-free one because the LTS 3 it adds cancels the saving.

- 287(2), Owner 2026-10-03, LONG-CALM r1 questions: "Free up to target (Recommended)": distance under the target is free; past it, 1 mi of LTS 3 must be saved per 2.5 mi, up to the 1.25x ceiling.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM, item 271): with a target, `refine.distance_charge_m` charges nothing up to it and the actual metres past it over `refine.WORTH_OVER_TARGET` (2.5); `presets.TARGET_CEILING_RATIO` (1.25) is the hard ceiling.

- 287(3), Owner 2026-10-03, LONG-CALM r1 questions: "Yes, calm and flagged (Recommended)": with a target well below the calm route, return the calm route flagged over target (e.g. Union->Penn at 47 mi gives 57.5 mi, 10.5 mi over).

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM, item 271): `routing._past_target` weighs the routes past the target within the ceiling against the calmest that fits by `refine.better`, and the calm route is answered with `calm_search.over_target_m`; the page says "X mi (Y km) over your target". Measured: Union Station to Penn at 47 mi answers 57.5 mi, 10.5 mi [16.9 km] over, with 0.26 mi of LTS 4.

- 287(4), Owner 2026-10-03, LONG-CALM r1 questions: "Loosen a bit (Recommended)": loosen the candidate near-tie stress bands (today 45 m LTS 4 / 300 m LTS 3) to about 150 m (500 ft) LTS 4 and 800 m (0.5 mi) LTS 3, so that more genuine alternates show, each with its stats. Measure how many of the 12 trips then offer an alternate.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-DEDODGE r1): `refine.ALT_TOP_BAND_M` 150 m [490 ft] and `ALT_SECOND_BAND_M` 800 m [0.5 mi]: a candidate's top figure (LTS 4, Avoid and red-junction cost) no more than 150 m above the answer's and its second (LTS 3 and orange-junction cost) no more than 800 m above. `ALT_OVERLAP` stays 0.70, the 5 mi different-road test, the ceiling, the target rule and the 250 hold are unchanged. Measured on the twelve Trailmaxxing trips: 2 offer a second route (Bethesda to the Capitol, 13.21 mi, LTS 3 0.47 mi, LTS 4 0, 6.12 mi of path, 0 red and 2 orange junctions, against the answer's 14.03 mi and 0.29 mi of LTS 3; Friendship Heights to Rosslyn, 8.82 mi, LTS 3 0.43 mi, LTS 4 0, 3.42 mi of path, 1 red and 3 orange, against 7.70 mi and 0.42 mi), against 1 at 45 m and 300 m.

**2026-10-03. FOLLOWUP-STRESS-SALIENCE, and what it leaves to the rebuild (OWNER-DECISIONS 274 to 283, 290, 292).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented (wip/stress-salience, 1ffe80c and the review revision 520914e; front end, plus the description's two new fields). docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Stress salience: the tiers' shapes and the facility rails", has the figures. Items 278, 281 and 282, and the data halves of 280 and 290, are not in this release: they are FOLLOWUP-NO-BIKE-PATHS and FOLLOWUP-OVERRIDE-REMATCH, for the next graph rebuild (293 (3)).

- 274, Owner 2026-10-03, on the map stress colours: "Looking at the new colors, LTS3 looks more distinctive than LTS4 and Avoid is hard to tell apart." Recorded with it: the default palette was LTS 3 #bf730b, LTS 4 #a32814 and Avoid #4a0810, two dark, low-saturation reds. Visual weight must rise with stress, and Avoid must differ from LTS 4 by a cue beyond hue, keeping 3:1 on the base map in both themes and the two-tone and colour-blind-friendly palettes with their tests; the owner sees a preview before deploy.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-STRESS-SALIENCE; `frontend/src/stressStyle.js`, `PALETTES` and `TIER_SHAPES`): in the default `blended` palette LTS 4 is a saturated red, #c80018 on its white casing (CIELAB chroma about 82, against the amber's 65), and Avoid a near-black, #14040a, on a coral-red casing, #ee3b2c. Avoid also differs from LTS 4 in shape and width: a dash-dot `[5, 1, 0.5, 1]` at 6.5 px against LTS 4's near-solid `[8, 1]` at 5 px, and a cross-hatch in its legend swatch and stress-bar segment. LTS 1 to 3 keep their colours. The two-tone and colour-blind-friendly palettes take the same widths and dashes with their own colours. Every tier stays 3:1 on the base map and both panels (the casing rule of `stressContrast.test.ts`). LTS 4 against Avoid is 38.9 CIEDE2000 apart under normal vision (20.9 before). The route's coloured sections widen with the tier too, and the route casing is 11 px. The owner saw the preview and approved it (297, below).

- 275, Owner 2026-10-03: "while they do have bike lanes, let's hide bike lanes on anything at least LTS4 by default and let a switch happen. Kenilworth avenue is hillarious at having a bike lane on it." Recorded with it: the painted-lane rail is hidden on LTS 4 and Avoid by default; a switch, off by default, "Show bike lanes on high-stress roads", shows them again; protected lanes and paths are unchanged; the facility bar and the description follow the same switch; display only.

  Implemented as: the switch is a `role="switch"` button after the Accessibility switch, off by default and remembered per browser (`routemaker.highStressLanes`). Off, the painted-lane layer's filter leaves out tiers 4 and up (`HIGH_STRESS_LANE_MIN_TIER`). The facility bar counts a painted lane on LTS 4 or Avoid as no facility. The description drops ", painted bike lane" from such a stretch: the API marks each stretch whose lane is entirely on LTS 4 or Avoid with `text_lanes_hidden`, and the front end uses it (it strips the words itself only for an older API). The stress ratings and the data are unchanged.

- 276, Owner 2026-10-03: "It's not easy to tell the difference between path, protected, and paintied. They are all jus various purples." Recorded with it: the three rails must differ at a glance by shape or pattern first, colour second, clear of the stress colours, the route blue and the unrated grey, at 3:1 on the base map; the legend and the facility bar match.

- 277, Owner 2026-10-03: "Let's try de-emphasizing painted even more. Paint isn't protection and lot's of maps overemphasize it." Amends 276: the painted-lane rail is the weakest cue (thinnest, lowest contrast still at 3:1, sparse dots, drawn below the others), listed last and labelled plainly.

  Implemented as (276 and 277; `FACILITIES` in `stressStyle.js`): a path is a solid violet rail (#4c1d95), 2.5 px each side. A protected lane is a magenta rail (#a21caf), 4 px each side, in short blocks like posts (`[0.3, 0.3]`). A painted lane is a 1 px sparse dotted rail (#9370f0, `[0.14, 0.4]`, 3.26:1 on the base map), 1.5 px with the Accessibility switch on and still the thinnest. The rails are drawn painted, then protected, then path, so paint lies lowest. The legend lists path, protected, then "Painted lane (no separation)". The facility bar takes its colours and patterns from the rails, so it no longer shares the stress colours.

- 278, Owner 2026-10-03: "there's still some paths you can't bike on included, like the paths at the zoo, and various hiking trails." FOLLOWUP-NO-BIKE-PATHS: paths where cycling is not allowed are neither routed nor drawn as bike facilities.

  Not in this release: deferred to FOLLOWUP-NO-BIKE-PATHS and the next graph rebuild (its rules are item 291).

- 279, Owner 2026-10-03: "There are a lot of trails that are now showing up as protected bike paths. Such as near lake accontik." Recorded with it: live.segment has no protected facility there, so the likely cause was the LTS 1 dash `[1]` showing the path rail through its gaps. The pattern a facility shows must never depend on the tier line drawn over it.

  Implemented as: no tier has an odd-length dash list any more. LTS 1 is solid (`dash: null`), and a test forbids odd lists. Each facility's pattern is its own over every tier, plain and strong, with the solid casing between the rail and the tier line. The suggested mechanism did not reproduce in MapLibre 6.11.2, where `[1]` drew solid. The legend's SVG swatch did draw `[1]` dashed, and that is fixed. With 290 (a), below, unpaved trails no longer carry a path's rails at all.

- 280, Owner 2026-10-03: "The cross county trail is unrideable in parts except by specific off road bikes." Recorded with it: folded into FOLLOWUP-NO-BIKE-PATHS. Mountain-bike-only sections must not be LTS 1 paths for the standard presets, nor routed by default, nor drawn as a bike path. Gravel and stone-dust trails stay rideable. Unpaved trails show as unpaved.

  Implemented as, the display part: a road or trail the tiles mark `unpaved` gets a dotted centre mark (`UNPAVED_DASH` `[1, 1.2]`), over its line, in its tier's casing colour (since 302, below, the unpaved casing under the brown). The mark is not drawn on faint lines or alleys, and the legend has an Unpaved entry. The description says ", unpaved" where half or more of a stretch is unpaved, and ", partly unpaved" where at least 0.1 mi is. Each entry carries a `surface` field (`routing.classify` reads `is_unpaved`). The routing part is deferred to FOLLOWUP-NO-BIKE-PATHS and the next rebuild.

- 281, Owner 2026-10-03, clarifying 280: "There are paved sections, but much of it is mountain bike trail." The Cross County Trail rule works section by section.

  Not in this release: deferred to FOLLOWUP-NO-BIKE-PATHS and the next graph rebuild.

- 282, Owner 2026-10-03: "Yes, queue both for the next rebuild." FOLLOWUP-OVERRIDE-REMATCH: (a) re-point the Baltimore Harford Rd override (way 424993005, split in the fresh extract) after checking the new ways against the Open Baltimore geometry; (b) the override loader re-matches a missing way by stored geometry and street name, only where that is unambiguous, reports every re-match and failure, and never silently drops an override.

  Not in this release: queued for the next graph rebuild, as the owner said.

- 283, Owner 2026-10-03: "The dashes in LTS4 still look less striking than LTS3. If I didn't see the key, I'd think that LTS4 was lower stress." Recorded with it: ink (the dash duty cycle) and width must rise with stress, LTS 4 near-solid and heavy, Avoid the heaviest with its own cue, so that the pattern alone reads low to high.

  Implemented as (`TIER_SHAPES`): ink, the share of the line drawn times its width, rises strictly: LTS 1 solid at 2.5 px (ink 2.50), LTS 2 `[4, 1]` at 3.25 px (2.60), LTS 3 `[2, 0.4]` at 4.25 px (3.54), LTS 4 `[8, 1]` at 5 px (4.44, 89% drawn) and Avoid `[5, 1, 0.5, 1]` at 6.5 px (4.77). Before, the figures were 3.00, 2.40, 1.75, 1.33 and 2.86. The duty cycle alone is not monotonic (LTS 1 is solid), and `stressSalience.test.ts` holds the rising ink in all three palettes. On the first preview the owner said "the colors look much clearer. That larger dash did it." (recorded with item 291).

- 290, Owner 2026-10-03, with a screenshot of Lake Accotink for 279 (no words of the owner's recorded): every trail there, including the dense mountain-bike singletrack west of the lake, was drawn as a light-green LTS 1 line with thick dark-purple path rails, reading as protected bike paths. Two fixes: (a) display: no prominent path rail on unpaved or natural-surface trails, which show faint with the unpaved mark only; (b) data, at the next rebuild (NO-BIKE-PATHS and SINGLETRACK-FIX): the singletrack becomes barred or MTB, faint and not routed for the standard presets.

  Implemented as, (a) only: the path rail's filter leaves out `unpaved == true`. A missing `unpaved` is an unknown surface and keeps its rails. The path rail went from 3 px to 2.5 px each side, lighter than the protected lane's 4 px. The legend says an unpaved trail has no path edges. Part (b) is deferred to the next rebuild.

- 292, Owner 2026-10-03, on the new colours: "that black in the LTS3 is a bit too harsh, especially with LTS4 being white." Recorded with it: soften LTS 3's near-black casing (#2b1a05), which shows in its dash gaps, keeping 3:1, the ink order and the greyscale and colour-blind separation. Apply the same check to every tier: a calmer tier must never carry a harsher gap contrast than a busier one.

  Implemented as (520914e): LTS 3's casing is a mid-dark amber-brown, #45290a, and its dash `[2, 0.4]` keeps the 8.5 px dash with a 1.7 px gap. Harshness, the gap share times the casing's contrast with the line (`gapHarshness`), is 0.60 for LTS 3 against LTS 4's 0.67 and Avoid's 1.34 in the default palette (LTS 3 was 1.51). LTS 3 is 3.60:1 on its casing, which is 12.0:1 on the base map. With the Accessibility switch on, a busy tier's casing (LTS 3 and up) is no longer pushed to black or white, though the switch still widens every line and casing. Two-tone's LTS 4 casing moved from #d42020 to #c81e1e. `stressSalience.test.ts` holds harshness non-decreasing from LTS 3 to LTS 4 to Avoid in every palette, plain and strong. LTS 2's gaps are not held to the rule: they are 0.90 in the default palette, harsher than LTS 3's, which is reported, not enforced. The owner accepted that as built (297).

**2026-10-04. The colour sign-off and the release review questions (OWNER-DECISIONS 297, 298).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 297, Owner 2026-10-04, on the revised colour preview (STRESS-SALIENCE r1, 520914e): "looks good". The colours are approved for release as shown. Read as accepting both open points as built, unless the owner says otherwise: LTS 2's gaps stay as they are (harshness 0.90 is reported, not enforced), and the strong accessibility switch widens lines but no longer blackens LTS 3-Avoid casings.

  Implemented as: no change; 520914e ships as shown in the preview.

- 298(1), Owner 2026-10-04, release review questions, the Default dodge rule: "Same as Trailmaxxing (Recommended)". Every preset, Default included, removes only dodges that avoid within the 50 m second-level tie step (as the top of the slider did). This amends 272's 0.25 mi rule; Konterra-type dodges, which avoid nothing, are still removed everywhere.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-DEDODGE; item 272, above): one rule on every ride type (`dedodge.TIE_RULE_ALL_PRESETS`). A dodge is taken out where it avoids no more than `dedodge.TIE_STEP_M`, 50 m [160 ft] (the second level's tie step, `refine.MAXCALM_STEPS[1]`), of the top and second figures together, with no turn charge. `dedodge.tie_rule()` reads the switch; set to False it restores 272's quarter mile (`MIN_AVOIDED_M`) and turn charge (`TURN_CHARGE_M`), which are kept as the documented fallback and still tested with the switch off, but decide no plan as shipped. The guards are unchanged: no removal adds LTS 4, Avoid or red-junction cost, makes the route longer, or makes it worse on the Hills slider's blended distance. Gap: a loop's dodges are not checked at all (`routing.py`, `if not loop`), so "every preset" holds for every ride type's point-to-point plans and not yet for loops.

- 298(2), Owner 2026-10-04, release review questions, no route within the 1.25x ceiling: "Calmest found, flagged (Recommended)". Return the calmest route found, flagged with how far over target it is; this amends the as-built "shortest".

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM; items 267 and 271, above): where no route the router gives is within the ceiling, `routing._past_target` answers the least stressful of all the routes found (the 258 to 262 order, then the shorter), where it answered the shortest; the shortest only where none can be read. Its readings stop at `refine.late_deadline` (the deadline less the trace reserve). The answer is flagged with `calm_search.over_target_m` and `calm_search.no_fit: true`, and the page's sentence that it is the least stressful found is then true. Gap: where none of the routes past the ceiling can be read, the shortest is answered, and the page still says "This is the least stressful one found" (`summary.ts`), which is then not shown to be so. `no_fit` is true only where no route within the target was found, false where one was, null with no target, and is cleared after the dodge pass only where the route now fits. `calm_search.limited: "target_distance"` is the no-fit answer's alone; the new `"ceiling"` means the exclusion search's next round found only routes past `ceiling_m`, so it stopped there (a calmer, longer route may exist, and the answer may well fit).

- 298(3), Owner 2026-10-04, release review questions, Hills set to seek hills: "Keep stress order + target (Recommended)". Seeking hills changes only the effort tiebreak (prefer climbing); the stress order (258-262) and the target (267-271) still apply.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-LONG-CALM; item 262, above): with the Hills slider seeking at the top of the stress slider, the stress-order search, the long calm plan and the target all run as at the detent (`long_calm_for` no longer takes a `seeking` argument). Only the third level's effort term inverts: `Context.hills_seek_weight`, the Hills position over 100, subtracts that share of the effort in `refine.level3`, so of two equally calm routes the one with more climbing is preferred. The 268 distance charge does not see that credit (`refine.charged_m`, the release re-check's S1): seeking climbs never buys miles, so without a target a longer, hillier route pays for its extra distance as at the detent, and under a target the miles are free as before. No climb search among the router's alternatives is asked there, and the answer's `hills_seek.limited` is `"calm_first"`; below the top of the stress slider the climb search is as before. FOLLOWUP-HILLS-TOLERATE (242) is still the later answer to what seeking should mean.

- 298(4), Owner 2026-10-04, release review questions, Mass Ride loops: "Keep excluded". Mass Ride stays point-to-point, with no loop control.

  Implemented as: an owner decision, not a change. `routing.loop_wanted` and the front end's loop control already leave a Mass Ride out of item 266, as built, and that is now the owner's choice rather than a developer's.

- 301, Owner 2026-10-04, on 300 (the Capital Bikeshare licence, which asks for no attribution): "Even if the license doesn't require crediting them, sources need citing." Standing rule: every data source is cited in the attribution and credits, whether or not its licence requires it. For bikeshare the map and route credits will carry a plain factual source citation, text only, with no logos, brand styling or implied affiliation, and the generic "Bikeshare" label stays in the controls. Audit the other sources to confirm each user-facing dataset is credited.

  Implemented as (the audit, whose credit wording 306 then shortened, below; the bikeshare citation waits for FOLLOWUP-BIKESHARE, 243 to 245 and 299, and is future work): `tests/test_credits.py` lists every source the route and place-search answers are made from (OSM, DDOT traffic volume and the CBD, VDOT, the Architect of the Capitol boundary, DC's Roadway Block, Open Baltimore, Montgomery Planning's LTS, USGS 3DEP; OSM and Photon for places) and holds `routing.ATTRIBUTION` and `geocode.ATTRIBUTION` to it both ways; the audit found nothing missing on the API side (it missed the U.S. Census Bureau's TIGER urban areas, which 306 then credited). On the map, `frontend/src/lib/credits.test.ts` holds the 11 map credits, which gained "Elevation and climb: U.S. Geological Survey 3D Elevation Program (3DEP)" and "Place search: Photon (komoot), Apache 2.0". The Arlington and Alexandria layers are internal comparisons only (items 152 to 155) and are tested to be cited nowhere.

- 302, Owner 2026-10-04: "For unpaved, instead of the stress colors we have earlier, just go with brown for everything, with darker shades for more traffic stress and the same dashes. I really doubt there are many gravel LTS4 roads." Recorded with it: unpaved roads and trails are drawn in one brown ramp, light to dark from LTS 1 to Avoid, with each tier's dash and width kept (283), on the map overlay and the route line's sections; a non-colour unpaved cue stays; 3:1 on the base map, each step distinguishable in greyscale and under colour-vision deficiency, unpaved LTS 1 distinct from the paved LTS 3 amber, the 292 harshness rule, and a CVD-safe version in the accessibility palettes; a preview for the owner before the deploy.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-STRESS-SALIENCE; `UNPAVED_PALETTES` in `frontend/src/stressStyle.js`; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Stress salience"): the default ramp is #d9b98c, #b58a55, #8c5e2e, #5e3a17, #33200d (L* 76.9 to 14.2), the light two on a dark brown casing (#3b2410) and the dark three on cream (#f6ead2); two-tone has a greyer taupe ramp (its paved LTS 3 is a yellow a warm tan would sit on) and `cvd` an ochre-olive ramp on the yellow side of the blue-yellow axis. The dotted unpaved mark stays as the cue that is not colour, drawn in the unpaved casing on the map and on the route (a new `route-unpaved` layer); the route line has twelve classes (five unpaved), each at least 3:1 on its halo. Every step is at least 1.5:1 in lightness and 10 CIEDE2000 from the last under every vision, every tier is at least 3:1 on every base-map surface (the worst, LTS 3, about 3.3:1), harshness rises from LTS 3 to Avoid (0.78, 0.94, 3.47), and unpaved LTS 1 is 19 or more CIEDE2000 from the paved amber under every vision. The weak spot: under deuteranopia unpaved LTS 1 and 2 are only about 5 to 6 CIEDE2000 from the paved green LTS 1 and 2, and the dotted mark tells them apart. The legend row reads "Brown, darker = busier". In the API each `stress_spans` entry says `unpaved` (true, false or null), and a section ends where the surface changes (additive). The preview is /home/steph/rmdata/demo/stress-salience-preview.html; the owner's look at it is still to come before the deploy.

- 303, Owner 2026-10-04, ARTERIAL dev questions: "Keep it LTS 4, and only lower ratings. In most cases, the smoothing is probably bunching by the intersection. Given that our routing is a sum of intersection stress and route stress, we don't want to double count."
  (1) Way 930215092 (N Capitol first-underpass east outer side lane) stays LTS 4, like the other side lanes.
  (2) AADT smoothing is lower-only. A smoothed count may replace a raw count only when it is lower, so a road's tier can fall but never rise because of smoothing. Rationale: high one-off counts usually bunch at intersections, and intersection stress is already charged separately (junction severity), so a high count on the link double-counts it.
