- 374, Owner 2026-10-04, loop clicks: "When make a loop is chosen, it should assume that the next point clicked is just a via point since it starts and stops at the same place. You shouldn't have to have 2 points in the same spot." With the loop toggle on, the start is also the finish. Every later point is a stop on the loop, and a click may land on the way back as well as the way out (insertion considers the closing leg). Points after the start are named as stops, not as an end.

  Implemented as (front end; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md, "Stops in a loop (OWNER-DECISIONS 374)"): with Make it a loop on, the first point is "Start and finish" and every later point a stop; `geo.addPoint` treats the closing leg as a slot, which wins a tie; search, rail cards, markers, the points list, announcements and the GPX use loop names, and a change that renames the points (the toggle, a ride type into or out of Mass Ride, an undo or redo) is announced; a new ride type keeps the toggle, and Mass Ride keeps it unused, so the next ride type brings the loop back; the toggle shows from one point. A drag on the route line's closing leg appends the stop, as a click does (`lineEdit.legPoints`, `insertIntoRide`). Reverse keeps the start and finish and reverses the stops; with a start and one stop it is aria-disabled and says why. The GPX names its points from the toggle the route shown was planned with, not the API's echo, and writes and reads `loop=1`, so a loop reopens as one. The request and links are unchanged, except that a Mass Ride's kept flag is not sent. A loop implied by an end on the start, with the toggle off, keeps Start and End. Not on a Mass Ride (298(4)), whose hints do not mention the toggle.

- 375, Owner 2026-10-04, zoomed-out trails: "Also zoomed out, can we stick to mostly the longer trails, it's getting messy." Below the busy-roads zoom, draw mainly the long trails. Compact park tangles and short stubs drop out until the rider zooms in. The exact measure and thresholds are a design choice, documented and tunable.

  Implemented as (ZOOMED-TRAILS; docs/OPERATIONS.md, "The long trails (z10-11)"): z10 and z11 are two tile levels (`core.stress_tiles.TRAILS`, `TRAILS_NEAR`), each keeping a path or trail only when `long_trails_predicate` says it is long: on a route relation of a high enough level for its surface (`segment.trail_route`: 0 none, 1 a local bicycle route or one with no network, 2 a long walking route, 3 a regional, national or international bicycle route), or in a named run (`segment.trail_run_m`: same-named drawn trail ways chained within 1,300 ft (400 m), name variants folded together, a way with no name named by its long route) of at least the level's bar for its surface (`pipeline.schema.Z11_PAVED_RUN_MI` and its siblings). A short bridge is judged as its trail (`segment.trail_bridge`; see 380). The columns are written by the rebuild (`pipeline.trail_routes`), so the rule takes effect with the next data rebuild; on a live table without all three of `trail_route`, `trail_run_m` and `trail_bridge` the tiles draw every trail as before, and the ETag names the columns (`t`, `l`, `b`). `FORMAT_VERSION` is 5; the api and pipeline images ship under one TAG, and VALIDATE refuses a build whose long-trail columns came out wrong (`pipeline.run.assert_long_trails`: the W&OD and C&O sentinel ways, row floors, no unjudged bridge). The front end draws the lines and rails thinner at z10-11 (`ZOOMED_OUT_SCALE`), with the casing edge, rails and unpaved mark never under a pixel and the accessibility switch still wider, and the legend says which trails are shown and that every other one shows from z12. Measured on a full-size copy of the 2026-10-03 build: 5,461 mi of trail drawn today at z10-11, 1,037 mi at z11 and 894 at z10.

- 377, Owner 2026-10-04, ZOOMED-TRAILS open questions: "Car free roads stay. The point at this zoom is to see what would be a great long distance trip." "Northwest branch trail stays, but keep as is. It's because it turns into a mtb trail fast." "Local trails only at higher zooms. Basically, at birds eye view I want to see a bicycle version of the interstate routes." At z10-11 timed car-free roads are kept, local bicycle routes (lcn) no longer qualify a way on their own, and the Northwest Branch result is accepted as it is.

  Implemented as: `long_trails_predicate(rule, car_free=True)` keeps any way with `car_free_when` set whatever its length (Sligo Creek Parkway on weekends); a local route is `trail_route` 1, under every bar (`PAVED_ROUTE_MIN` 2), and its relation's name is not used to name a way, so it cannot keep one through a run either; the Northwest Branch Trail is left as the data has it (dropped at z10-11: its unpaved ways are in an mtb relation). 377's "walking-route handling (z11 yes, z10 no) is unchanged" is overridden by 378 for unpaved ways: a walking route keeps a paved way only, at both zooms.

- 378, Owner 2026-10-04: "Patapsco Traverse appears to be a mountain bike trail. Make sure to not include those." At z10-11 a walking route no longer qualifies an unpaved way at either zoom, and mountain-bike trails never qualify, whatever their name or route.

  Implemented as: an unpaved way needs `trail_route` 3 (a regional-or-larger bicycle route) or the unpaved run bar at both zooms; a way in a route relation whose `route` includes mtb (`hiking;mtb` too), or tagged `mtb:scale` 1 or more, `mtb=designated` or `mtb:type`, is written with route 0 and no name (`pipeline.trail_routes.is_mountain_bike_way`, `read_routes`), so it qualifies by neither and adds nothing to a run. The tag rule exempts a paved way, as `routemaker.singletrack` reads one (an `mtb:scale` on asphalt: Upper Rock Creek, Northwest Branch, Cross County); on the 2026-10-03 extract the exemption changes nothing, since every flagged drawn way is flagged by its relation. Measured: 758 mountain-bike rows drawn today, 0 kept at z10 or z11; the Patapsco Traverse and the Howard County Thru Trail are gone at both zooms.

- 380, Owner 2026-10-04, on Grist Mill (it measures 2.54 mi in OSM): "Yes, bring it in". The z11 paved named-run bar drops from 3 mi to 2.5 mi. This keeps Grist Mill at z11 and adds about 39 region miles (Oxon Run, Arlington Boulevard, Easton Rails-to-Trails, etc.). The z10 bars and the unpaved bars are unchanged.

  Implemented as: `Z11_PAVED_RUN_MI = 2.5` (4,023 m); the other bars stay 5 mi paved at z10, and 5 and 8 mi unpaved. Measured: the Grist Mill Trail keeps 2.54 of its 2.54 mi at z11 and none at z10. The short-bridge rule beside it is the orchestrator's decision, not the owner's: the trail's two wooden bridges (`surface=wood`, read as unpaved) left holes in it at z11, so a drawn trail way tagged bridge=*, or a chain of them whose ends meet, of at most 330 ft (100 m) in all with a trail way at each outer end, takes the greater of its own route and run and the lower of its ends', and is judged on its trail's surface, not its deck's (`segment.trail_bridge` 1 or 2; `pipeline.trail_routes.judge_bridges`). It keeps no bridge on its own and lowers none.

- 381, Owner 2026-10-04, permanently car-free roads at z10-11: "I think off is fine here. Nobody routes around the tiny roads." Only timed car-free roads are exempt from the zoomed-out bars (377). Roads closed to cars for good are judged like trails: a qualifying route or named run, otherwise shown from z12. The docs should say so.

  Implemented as: no code change; `long_trails_predicate` exempts only `car_free_when`, and a road closed for good is a path judged by its route and run like any other (tested: `test_a_road_closed_to_cars_for_good_is_judged_like_a_trail`). docs/OPERATIONS.md, "The long trails", and the `pipeline.schema` comment say so. Measured: of 21.5 mi of roads closed for good, 0.8 mi are kept at z11 and 0.8 at z10; every timed one (6.8 mi) is kept.

- 382, Owner 2026-10-04, beta problem reports: "It's just going to be me. I'll probably make a discord community to involve others." At first the owner is the only tester. The banner names no channel ("tell the person who gave you access"). A report link, such as a future Discord invite, is an optional build setting shown only when set, so adding Discord later needs no code change. The tester handout and the accessible password format still ship, ready for when others join.

  Implemented as: the notice says "If anything looks wrong, tell the person who gave you access." `ship-data.sh --report-url https://...` sets VITE_BETA_REPORT_URL, and only then does the notice show a "Report a problem" link (https only, `betaReportUrl` in `frontend/src/lib/betaBanner.ts`). The handout is `docs/BETA-TESTER-HANDOUT.md`; `scripts/beta/make-htpasswd.sh` writes passwords of lowercase letters and digits without look-alikes, in four dash-separated groups of five.

- 384, Owner 2026-10-05, the sidebar's open questions (312): "1 system, 2 settings, 3 off, 4. call it high contrast, 5 yes".
  1. Theme: follow the system's light or dark setting (as built).
  2. The fourth bottom-bar button is "Settings", not "About" (the bar has five buttons since 392/393, which put Plan first). It holds the sign-in note and the display options.
  3. The planner zoom notice stays off; the notice lives in the Map layers sheet.
  4. The "Accessibility" switch is renamed "High contrast", with neutral wording that drops "Color-blind friendly". The main-panel shortcut stays off; the switch also appears in Settings.
  5. Download the Atkinson Hyperlegible font (regular and bold woff2, plus its SIL OFL licence) and switch it on, credited in licenses.txt.

  Implemented as (wip/sidebar-384, front end only; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md "The sidebar (312)"):
  1. No change: the theme follows `prefers-color-scheme`; a test holds that there is no theme switch.
  2. "About" became "Settings" (`BAR_ITEMS` id `settings`, the sheet "Settings", `SHEET_TITLES.settings`, a gear icon). The sheet holds a "Display" group with the High contrast switch, then the existing sign-in note in its own "Signing in" section; no other settings were invented. The sheet's focus and return behaviour are unchanged, and the tests are renamed.
  3. `PLANNER_EXTRAS.zoomNotice` stays false (a test pins it).
  4. The switch reads "High contrast" in the Map layers sheet, the Settings sheet, the switched-off planner shortcut (`AccessibilityShortcut` became `HighContrastShortcut`, `PLANNER_EXTRAS.highContrastShortcut`), the docs, the tests and `scripts/a11y/check.mjs`. Its hint is "Bolder lines, stronger borders and text, and colors that don't rely on red and green. Kept in this browser." The internal names, the `routemaker.accessibility` storage key, the `a11y` root class and the `palette=` link values are unchanged, so every existing share link decodes the same (`planHash` and the palette parameter were not touched). It is one state shown twice; the component takes an `idBase` so the two copies' ids are unique (`a11y-*` in Map layers, `settings-contrast-*` in Settings), and a test and the browser check hold that.
  5. The two woff2 files (sha256 `d64ba838...`, `140e2bd2...`) and `OFL.txt` (`f32d22b3...`), already downloaded and verified, were copied into `frontend/src/fonts/`; `main.tsx` imports `./fonts/fonts.css` after `./styles.css`. The build emits `/assets/atkinson-hyperlegible-regular-<hash>.woff2` and `...-bold-<hash>.woff2`, and the built CSS names only those. The `licenceNotices` plugin adds "Atkinson Hyperlegible - 2020 (OFL-1.1)" and the full OFL text to `licenses.txt` (tested); docs/SOURCES.md records the font in a section with no `Credit:` line (it is a bundled asset, credited in licenses.txt as 384 says, not a data credit on the map).
  Also (the sidebar mutation re-check's nits A-D, `SIDEBAR-recheck-mutation.md`): App's dispatch from the focus decision (the legend target, the error branch, the Back click) is pinned, and `barCurrent` is a pure function over every view and button; the Junctions fold's guard, its count and the 0 edges (`foldTitle("x", 0)`, `stepsCount(0)`) are tested; the beta-banner CSS guard also covers `visibility: hidden` and `opacity: 0`; a regex pins that Copy link is unpinned while a sheet is open.
- 388, 389, Owner 2026-10-05, "Make it a loop": it sits by the search, not under the Ride line's Edit, and appears before a start is placed (except on Mass Ride), with "Place the starting point, then a stop or two along the way." once it is checked.

  Implemented as (wip/loop-plan, front end only; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md "Make it a loop by the search"):
  - The checkbox left `DialsPanel` and is in the Points section (`App.tsx`, `.loop-toggle`), under the search and above the points list, outside the part that hides while the points are compact, so it stays when a route is shown. It is `loopView` (`lib/loop.ts`), which no longer needs a point; its toggle goes through `commitDials`, so `loopChangeSaid` still speaks on a toggle, a ride-type change and an undo or redo. `withLoop` moved to `lib/loop.ts`; `DialsPanel` lost its `points` prop. The Ride summary is unchanged and still says the loop.
  - Checked with no point, the box's visible hint (and its description) is `LOOP_FIRST_HINT`; the same words, after "Loop on.", are said once (`LOOP_FIRST_SAID`, from `loopChangeSaid` with no points and the loop coming on; "Loop off." when it goes off, pressed or undone; nothing when it did not change). Unchecked with no point, the hint is the start-alone one. The id of the hint is a `useId()`.
  - `emptyPlanHint` and `loneStartHint` no longer point at "the ride settings (the Ride line's Edit button)": they say to check "Make it a loop" under the search.
  - The row is 44 px high (`.loop-toggle .toggle`).

- 392, 393, Owner 2026-10-05, getting back to the planner: "Plan button is probably best". A "Plan" button first in the bottom bar, current on the planner; the sheets' Back reads "Back to planner"; no title link.

  Implemented as (wip/loop-plan, front end only; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md "The sidebar (312)", the Plan button):
  - `BAR_ITEMS` has `plan` first (`opens: "planner"`, so `barCurrent` marks it while the planner shows and no other button then); five buttons. Pressing it sets the view to the planner with cause `planButton`; `focusOnViewChange` returns `{ kind: "planner" }` and App focuses the planner's heading, the `h1` "RouteMaker" (`PLANNER_TITLE`, `tabIndex={-1}`), and scrolls the panel to the top. Back and Escape still return the focus to the bar button that opened the sheet.
  - Reading of 392 (the orchestrator's, for the owner to confirm): the Plan button puts the focus on the planner's heading, the `h1`, because it goes to the planner as a whole (the phone header's "Show planner" leaves the focus on the toggle, below); Back and Escape return the focus to the bar button that opened the sheet, because they undo that opening. Plan on the planner already focuses the heading and scrolls the panel to the top.
  - The phone header's toggle (it was "Hide"/"Plan") reads "Hide planner" / "Show planner", with `aria-expanded` and `aria-controls` as before; "Show planner" always shows the planner, even if a sheet was open when the panel was hidden; it leaves the focus on the toggle (cause `panelToggle`), the heading focus being the Plan button's.
  - The bar's hints are a sibling `hidden` span of each button, kept as its `aria-describedby`, so the name is exactly the label and the hint is heard once. The Plan hint reads "Shows the planner: ..." (consistent with "Back to planner").
  - The sheets' Back button shows "Back to planner" (`BACK_LABEL`) beside the arrow, in visible words; its `aria-label` is gone, so the name and the label are one.
  - At a phone's width the five buttons share the bar with the icon over the words, which wrap ("Map layers" on two lines); each is 48 px high and 64 px wide at 320 px. The Back button is 44 px high. Checked at 320 and 375 px in scripts/a11y/check.mjs.

- 394, Owner 2026-10-05, on the elevation chart and the Mass Ride flow model (ELEVATION-CHART-dev.md): fold placement "keep both" (the "Elevation and stress" fold sits above "Stress and facilities"); flow constants: "the numbers sound about right. We have literature to check that plus video recordings of events in DC that seem to indicate that it's a reasonable number." `routemaker.flow` is accepted as the working model (about 99 riders a minute per 11 ft lane on the flat; climbs slow to 1/(1+12x(grade-1%)); descents past 4% cut the density by 1/(1+5x(grade-4%))). Backlog: FOLLOWUP-FLOW-CALIBRATION.

  Status: applied (wip/elevation-chart): both folds kept; the flow module, this PLAN and docs/DEVELOPMENT.md say "accepted as the working model; sources pending FOLLOWUP-FLOW-CALIBRATION", and docs/SOURCES.md has the pending entry.

- 395, Owner 2026-10-05, "Use my location" as a starting point (FOLLOWUP-CURRENT-LOCATION, front end only): a button beside the search and a "Your location" choice; one look-up per press; set as the start or the next point, announced, approximate and draggable; plain messages for each failure; links keep full precision with a one-line note; HTTPS only; screen readers first; US units.

  Implemented as (wip/current-location, front end only, based on main at f0f5fc6, 2026-10-05; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md "Use my location"; the server-side log changes the review found are on their own branch, wip/privacy-logs, PLAN 401):
  - `lib/geolocation.ts` (injectable `GeoEnv`, one `getCurrentPosition` with high accuracy, 10 s, 30 s; no `watchPosition`), `PlaceSearch.tsx` (the button, the list entry), `App.tsx` (`useMyLocation`, the notices, the link note), `MapView.tsx` (the accuracy circle).
  - Insecure context or no geolocation: the button is `aria-disabled` with its reason as description and visible text, not removed.
  - Which points came from the location is memory only: a list of point objects (`fromHere`), never in the link, storage or a log; the point itself is in the link at full precision, as any point. Copy link says "This link includes your location as the start." (the button is described by it, and a press says it after "Link copied.").
  - Developer's extension of 395, owner to confirm: when only a later point came from the location, Copy link says "This link includes your location as a point on the route." (395 names the start only; the link carries the rider's spot either way).
  - Drag rule (review, 2026-10-05): dragging a point that came from the location keeps the note, erring toward telling the rider; undo restores it. A reload or the sign-in round trip loses it.
  - The "Your location" choice in the search list follows the Start / Destination / Stop choice; the button goes in like a map click. An app-side watchdog ends a look-up the browser never answers; where the browser has the Permissions API, an open permission prompt does not count against it (up to 60 s), and its clock starts again when the prompt is answered.
  - Sign-in round trip: the plan, location included, is kept in this tab's sessionStorage for the round trip only and read once. This is the accepted exception to "never stored" (OWNER-DECISIONS 398, "yes, keep location"): `SKIP_SIGN_IN_PLAN_WITH_LOCATION` in lib/signIn.ts stays false (flipping it also needs the Settings sign-in sentence and two test pins changed; the comment at the switch says which).

- 396, Owner 2026-10-05, on how "major" intersections are counted on the Mass Ride chart (333): "Similar rules to other riding. Go by stress ratings of the roads. They should be similar in most cases." This replaces 333's lane-count rule. A junction is major when the crossed or joined road is rated LTS 3 or higher, whatever the control (signal, stop or none), or when the junction itself carries a stress rating. Corkers are needed at the major junctions (142). No lane counting and no "any stop sign" rule. Busy roads with no event in group mode (a signalised right turn onto an arterial, or riding along a busy road past a busy cross street) must still be marked.

  Status: built (wip/elevation-chart, `routemaker.intersections.major_crossings`), awaiting review. 400 confirms its corker rule for turns onto a busy road. Where finding the busy-road majors fails, the chart says once (summary, table caption) that only the higher or very high stress junctions were found, so the list may be incomplete (`crossings_complete: false`), and never "no major intersections ahead".

- 397, Owner 2026-10-05, on the Avoid colour (325, route chart): "Avoid as a single color should be magenta. It's a very striking danger color. Only do if a route uses it". Avoid is drawn in a single magenta, replacing the chart's coral (#f4a6a0), and appears (with its key entry) only when the route actually uses an Avoid stretch. Reading recorded: this applies to the route chart and the route line; the map's general Avoid roads are not repainted magenta by this decision. Magenta must still pass the a11y rules: not colour alone (keep the AVOID word / its own texture), contrast against neighbours, and a high-contrast-mode mapping.

  Status: built (wip/elevation-chart): #d6008f on the chart's riders track (`profileChart.AVOID_FILL`) and on the route's own Avoid sections, paved and unpaved (`routeColours.ROUTE_AVOID_MAGENTA`, over a near-black halo, 4.1:1), in every palette; the map's Avoid roads keep the palette's Avoid. On the chart: a white AVOID (4.9:1) at the 11-unit type, or "A" on a narrow block (at least 10 units wide), dark chevrons of its own, and a two-tone frame (near-black outside, white inside) so one tone is 3:1 from any band or panel it touches; no single fill can be 3:1 from every band. The chart's key ("Avoid (A where narrow): no carrying capacity") and the route legend list Avoid only when the route rides some. Forced colours keep the magenta and map the outer frame to the system's text colour and the inner one to its background. The route panel's stress bar and the route chart's stress strip draw Avoid in the same magenta, with the bar's Avoid cross-hatch in near-black (`stressBar.stressSegments`), and the route line's paved Avoid carries a white dash-dot down its middle (`routeColours.ROUTE_AVOID_MARK`, MapView's route-avoid layer; the legend swatch shows it), so in the high contrast palette, where the magenta is close to LTS 3 and LTS 2 for some colour visions, it is not told by colour alone; an unpaved Avoid keeps its dotted unpaved mark. A path rated Avoid is drawn as Avoid, not traffic-free.

- 399, Owner 2026-10-05, on the route chart scrub: "auto pan". Keep the map auto-panning to follow the chart marker as the rider scrubs (mouse, touch and keyboard).

  Status: kept as built (wip/elevation-chart, `MapView` eases the map to the scrub marker when it leaves the screen); no change.

- 400, Owner 2026-10-05, on corkers at turns onto busy roads (396 vs 142): asked whether a right turn onto an LTS 3+ road with no lanes crossed needs corkers: "That would need a corker. It's equivalent to a right turn". Reading recorded: every major junction under 396 (crossing, left turn or right turn onto an LTS 3+ road) says "corkers needed", and the future corker count (PLAN "Corkers needed") counts these turns as well as crossings. 142's "only needed across lts 3+ roads" means the busy road's tier sets the need, not whether lanes are crossed.

  Status: the chart already did this (`Major.corkers_needed`: the crossed or joined road is LTS 3 or worse); the API's description now says so, and PLAN "Corkers needed" records that the count includes the turns. A junction the planner flags where the road the junction is about (the crossed or joined road) is LTS 1-2 still says "no corkers needed" (142: the busy road's tier sets the need). In practice this does not arise on a Mass Ride: the group planner flags only junctions about an LTS 3+ road.

- 401, Owner 2026-10-05, on Valhalla's slow-request logging (the privacy review of 395): "Yes" to keeping it off; the server-side log changes ship in their own small branch, split out of wip/current-location. 395's privacy clause is the reason: the rider's position is "never stored or logged beyond the route request, like any clicked point".

  Implemented as (wip/privacy-logs, server side only, based on main at f0f5fc6, 2026-10-05; docs/DEVELOPMENT.md "Logs and the rider's position"):
  - Valhalla: loki's and thor's `logging.long_request` is 3,600,000 ms (`NEVER_LONG_MS`, scripts/build_valhalla_configs.py), far past httpd's 30 s timeout, so the slow-request warning, which can carry the request's locations, never fires. Cost: Valhalla's slow-request warnings are lost for every route; route timing is read from the app's own timing instead (gunicorn's access log has the duration, `%(D)s`, and nginx's has `$request_time`). The time is the whole plan request's, not each router call's (home's Caddy keeps no access log). Needs a deploy and a router restart: on the live stack `docker compose restart valhalla-standard valhalla-no-trail valhalla-ebike valhalla-weekend` (restart, not `up -d`); on the beta `scripts/beta/beta-compose.sh restart` of the same four.
  - Beta nginx: the access log, `/var/log/nginx/rmbeta-access.log`, uses `rmbeta_noquery`: the method, the path (`$uri`), the status, the size and the time. No query string, no Referer, no client address, no tester name. `/api/reverse` and `/api/geocode`, whose queries hold a location, log nginx errors at `crit` only, to `/var/log/nginx/rmbeta-error.log`, so an upstream 502/504 line no longer records their query (review addition, owner to confirm: this loses nginx's upstream diagnostics for those two paths, and so does dropping the client address and tester name from the access log). docs/BETA-RUNBOOK.md names both files in its footprint, undo and Logs lines.
  - Open: Photon's own request logging is unverified (check `docker logs photon` after one search on a dev stack; add a log4j override if queries show); whether Valhalla logs a request on an error path (e.g. no suitable edges), and the app's own log of Valhalla's refusal text (src/core/routing.py), and whether the route request should send `"do_not_track": true`; a `Permissions-Policy: geolocation=(self)` header at the edge.

**2026-10-03. Arterial calibration and the override re-match (OWNER-DECISIONS 282, 284, 285, 286, 294, 295, 296; 303, 2026-10-04, amends the smoothing).** The owner's words, verbatim as recorded, then what was implemented.

- 282: "Yes, queue both for the next rebuild." (a) Re-point the Baltimore Harford Rd override (way 424993005) at the extract's new ways, verified against the Open Baltimore geometry; (b) override loader robustness: re-match a row whose way is missing by stored geometry plus street name, only when unambiguous, report every re-match and every failure, never drop a row silently, store a fingerprint with each row. Loading overrides into the live DB stays under the owner-approval rule.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-OVERRIDE-REMATCH): every row of `fixtures/overrides/` carries a fingerprint, and `pipeline.rematch` re-points a missing row at APPLY_OVERRIDES only when every test holds (name and class, within 20 ft (6 m) of the stored line and within 30 degrees of its direction, 90% coverage, the stored length within a factor of 1.25, no side-by-side pair, no overreaching same-name way, no collision); the rebuild writes `override-rematch.md`/`.csv` (docs/OPERATIONS.md). Harford Road: the 2026-10-03 extract redrew the 43 ft (13.2 m) way as 213 ft (64.8 m) of new ways, 1562097553 and 1562097555 (the two carriageways of a short divided section) and 1562097556; the file re-points the row at all three, checked against record 634. The generic re-match declines it, as it should, so it stays unapplied until the owner approves loading the file and deletes database row 424993005 (OPERATIONS rebuild checklist).

- 284: "North Capitol street is underrated. There's definitely parts that I'd put at Avoid, but it's listed as LTS3."

- 285: "Why is first street NW between Q and Florida LTS3. It's a fairly low stress road."

- 286: "At around M street heading north it changes into an undertunnel, those parts are avoid. The side streets would be LTS4, and on the pickup. When it does it again, the underparts are still 4 with the sides being LTS3. Narrower, so less stress. It may say 25, but it's treated like a highway."

- 294: "I wouldn't change them. It's mostly a specific road. For instance, Virginia Ave goes under the road but is fine due to a protected bike lane."

- 295: "I'm not crazy enough to ride my bike there. Haven't tried." 286's targets stand as the owner's judgement, not ride-tested.

- 296: "Let's keep the current rules as is. There's a lot of good alternatives in the area anyway." No volume lift of multilane arterials; what remains is the North Capitol corridor and same-street AADT smoothing, a data-quality fix the owner can veto.

  Implemented as (FOLLOWUP-ARTERIAL-CALIBRATION): the North Capitol named corridor (`fixtures/corridors/`, `routemaker.corridors`), matched by name and position, never by way id: first underpass through lanes Avoid, its side lanes and the pickup LTS 4 (way 468472149 a side lane), second underpass lanes LTS 4 and its sides LTS 3; protected lanes, separate bikeways and paths exempt; not a generic underpass rule. Same-street AADT smoothing (`pipeline.aadt_smoothing`): 1st St NW way 483241819 goes from LTS 3 to LTS 2 with no threshold changed. The veto is `RebuildContext.smooth_volume` (docs/OPERATIONS.md).

**2026-10-04. Lower-only smoothing (OWNER-DECISIONS 303; amending 296).**

- 303: "Keep it LTS 4, and only lower ratings. In most cases, the smoothing is probably bunching by the intersection. Given that our routing is a sum of intersection stress and route stress, we don't want to double count." Way 930215092 stays LTS 4 like the other side lanes; a smoothed count replaces a raw one only when it is lower.

  Implemented as: smoothing replaces a count only where the median is lower, so a tier can fall and never rise. Following the owner's reason (ARTERIAL review r0, SF1; for the owner to confirm), only the link reads the median: the segment table publishes the agency's count (`volume_aadt`) and keeps the tier on that count (`stress_unsmoothed_tier`), and the junction model reads both, so the volume bunched at an intersection is charged at the intersection and not twice. At 1st St NW the link is LTS 2 and the Q St junction is still rated on DDOT's 10,665 and LTS 3.
